- 2:30/q07/p52 God mentioned Adam’s future vicegerency before his creation to honor him and ensure the angels would revere him if He brought him into being. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (author): God mentioned Adam’s future vicegerency before his creation to honor him and ensure the angels would revere him if He brought him into being. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD]
- 2:30/q07/p53 God meant to tell the angels that Adam would inhabit earth, even though his initial creation took place in heaven. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (author): God meant to tell the angels that Adam would inhabit earth, even though his initial creation took place in heaven. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD]
- 2:30/q07/p54 Others put prophethood first because reason alone cannot establish angels; revelation, grounded in knowledge of prophethood, is the route to affirming them. — RAZI-FULL (بعض الناس): Others put prophethood first because reason alone cannot establish angels; revelation, grounded in knowledge of prophethood, is the route to affirming them. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(بعض الناس)]
- 2:30/q07/p55 Rationalists agree that angels ennoble the upper world, just as humans ennoble the lower world. — RAZI-FULL (author): Rationalists agree that angels ennoble the upper world, just as humans ennoble the lower world. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p56 Philosophers affirm rational proofs of angels' existence; Razi says he has deep, subtle discussions of their arguments, while other people offer merely persuasive proofs. — RAZI-FULL (الفلاسفة): Philosophers affirm rational proofs of angels' existence; Razi says he has deep, subtle discussions of their arguments, while other people offer merely persuasive proofs. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(الفلاسفة)]
- 2:30/q07/p57 One proof divides living beings into mortal rational humans, mortal nonrational beasts, and rational immortal angels; if wisdom creates the lower and middle ranks, it should create the highest too. — RAZI-FULL (بعض الناس): One proof divides living beings into mortal rational humans, mortal nonrational beasts, and rational immortal angels; if wisdom creates the lower and middle ranks, it should create the highest too. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(بعض الناس)]
- 2:30/q07/p58 The Throne's height is beyond conception and its ascent takes fifty thousand years, yet angels descend in an instant. — RAZI-FULL (author): The Throne's height is beyond conception and its ascent takes fifty thousand years, yet angels descend in an instant. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p59 Other angels manage the affairs of the world; Razi connects them with the angels ranged in ranks, those scattering, and those apportioning commands. — RAZI-FULL (author): Other angels manage the affairs of the world; Razi connects them with the angels ranged in ranks, those scattering, and those apportioning commands. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p60 Ibn Abbas says angels besides the guardians record leaves falling from trees; in a desert, a person who feels movement should call on God's servants for help. — RAZI-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says angels besides the guardians record leaves falling from trees; in a desert, a person who feels movement should call on God's servants for help. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q07/p61 Angels' nearness to God cannot mean nearness in place or direction; it means nearness in rank and honor. — RAZI-FULL (author): Angels' nearness to God cannot mean nearness in place or direction; it means nearness in rank and honor. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p62 An opponent says the angels' confession that they know only what God taught resembles an apology and implies a prior offense. — RAZI-FULL (المخالف): An opponent says the angels' confession that they know only what God taught resembles an apology and implies a prior offense. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(المخالف)]
- 2:30/q07/p63 Although 66:6 concerns Hell's keepers, 16:50 is general: angels do everything commanded, including avoiding prohibitions, since refraining is commanded too. — RAZI-FULL (author): Although 66:6 concerns Hell's keepers, 16:50 is general: angels do everything commanded, including avoiding prohibitions, since refraining is commanded too. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p64 Angels could not rightly criticize human sin if they themselves sinned. — RAZI-FULL (author): Angels could not rightly criticize human sin if they themselves sinned. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p65 In 74:31, أصحاب النار means Hell's keepers, controllers, and administrators, not angels being punished. — RAZI-FULL (author): In 74:31, أصحاب النار means Hell's keepers, controllers, and administrators, not angels being punished. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p66 If the angels were grieving, the answer reassures them that there are many devout people whom God will not disappoint. — RAZI-FULL (author): If the angels were grieving, the answer reassures them that there are many devout people whom God will not disappoint. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p67 Mujahid says the angels purify themselves of sins and errors in pursuit of God’s pleasure. — RAZI-FULL (مجاهد): Mujahid says the angels purify themselves of sins and errors in pursuit of God’s pleasure. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(مجاهد)]
- 2:30/q07/p68 God answers the angels through knowledge rather than power, will, hearing, sight, or other attributes, showing knowledge’s superior rank among divine perfections. — RAZI-FULL (author): God answers the angels through knowledge rather than power, will, hearing, sight, or other attributes, showing knowledge’s superior rank among divine perfections. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p69 God does not appoint an angel as prophet because people cannot receive His effusion and command directly; perfected prophets receive angels, while the highest may speak to God without an intermediary, as Moses and Muhammad did. — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): God does not appoint an angel as prophet because people cannot receive His effusion and command directly; perfected prophets receive angels, while the highest may speak to God without an intermediary, as Moses and Muhammad did. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p70 God’s reply asserts knowledge of the wisdom the angels had not grasped, including the good achieved through the human composite and its disciplined powers. — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): God’s reply asserts knowledge of the wisdom the angels had not grasped, including the good achieved through the human composite and its disciplined powers. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p71 It may refer specifically to Adam, whose mention stands for his descendants, or generally to one who succeeds the angels. — NASAFI-FULL (author): It may refer specifically to Adam, whose mention stands for his descendants, or generally to one who succeeds the angels. [1 notes: NASAFI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p72 One explanation is that God has a detailed wisdom for creating this kind despite the harms the angels mentioned, which they do not know. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (قيل): One explanation is that God has a detailed wisdom for creating this kind despite the harms the angels mentioned, which they do not know. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q07/p73 God had informed the angels what Adam’s descendants would do, so they made this statement. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (السدي عن ابن عباس وابن مسعود وغيرهما من الصحابة): God had informed the angels what Adam’s descendants would do, so they made this statement. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(السدي عن ابن عباس وابن مسعود وغيرهما من الصحابة)]
- 2:30/q07/p74 Mujahid says the angels mean that they exalt and magnify God. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (مجاهد): Mujahid says the angels mean that they exalt and magnify God. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(مجاهد)]
- 2:30/q07/p75 The account displays Adam's distinction through knowledge, the angels' prostration, his residence in Paradise, and God's provision of repentance after his lapse. — BIQAI-FULL (author): The account displays Adam's distinction through knowledge, the angels' prostration, his residence in Paradise, and God's provision of repentance after his lapse. [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p76 Mujahid says the angels mean that they magnify and exalt God. — DURR-FULL (مجاهد): Mujahid says the angels mean that they magnify and exalt God. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(مجاهد)]
- 2:30/q07/p77 Al-Hasan says angels worried earth could not hold Adam and his descendants; God answered that He would appoint death, then hope. — DURR-FULL (الحسن): Al-Hasan says angels worried earth could not hold Adam and his descendants; God answered that He would appoint death, then hope. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q07/p78 The continuation of the Mathnawi image compares the angels’ forbearance to foam on a sea of divine forbearance: foam passes, while the sea remains. — BURSEVI (author): The continuation of the Mathnawi image compares the angels’ forbearance to foam on a sea of divine forbearance: foam passes, while the sea remains. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q07/p79 The people of God describe the first manifestation as al-ʿamāʾ; its light disclosed the entranced angels, who preceded the natural bodily world and all other creation. — ALUSI-FULL (أهل الله): The people of God describe the first manifestation as al-ʿamāʾ; its light disclosed the entranced angels, who preceded the natural bodily world and all other creation. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(أهل الله)]
- 2:30/q07/p80 The account says God continually creates angels from the world's breaths for as long as its inhabitants breathe. — ALUSI-FULL (أهل الله): The account says God continually creates angels from the world's breaths for as long as its inhabitants breathe. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(أهل الله)]
- 2:30/q07/p81 Al-Ālūsī's own account divides angels into two groups: those absorbed in knowing God and those charged with managing affairs by divine decree. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Al-Ālūsī's own account divides angels into two groups: those absorbed in knowing God and those charged with managing affairs by divine decree. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p82 The prefixed lām marks transmission to the angels. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The prefixed lām marks transmission to the angels. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p83 Alternatively, God may have introduced Adam to the angels so they could recognize his rank, since his divine image concealed his inward reality from the upper host except the Tablet and Pen. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Alternatively, God may have introduced Adam to the angels so they could recognize his rank, since his divine image concealed his inward reality from the upper host except the Tablet and Pen. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p84 The apparent reading is that the same angels utter both clauses. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The apparent reading is that the same angels utter both clauses. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p85 If the material cosmos is one heaven and other heavens exist beyond it, the reality and stations of angels extend to inconceivable heights and depths. — ELMALILI (author): If the material cosmos is one heaven and other heavens exist beyond it, the reality and stations of angels extend to inconceivable heights and depths. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q07/p86 The immediate purpose is specifically to establish and manifest the wisdom and causes of Adam's fitness for vicegerency, which the angels had not grasped. — ELMALILI (author): The immediate purpose is specifically to establish and manifest the wisdom and causes of Adam's fitness for vicegerency, which the angels had not grasped. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q07/p87 The nominal sentence marks the angels' enduring disposition; fronting we may suggest exclusive constancy, but the author considers simple emphasis more likely. — IBNASHUR-FULL (author): The nominal sentence marks the angels' enduring disposition; fronting we may suggest exclusive constancy, but the author considers simple emphasis more likely. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p88 The setting implies the angels claimed the vicegerency and assumed Adam lacked it; testing their knowledge shows Adam's fitness and their lack of it. — TABATABAI (author): The setting implies the angels claimed the vicegerency and assumed Adam lacked it; testing their knowledge shows Adam's fitness and their lack of it. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q07/p89 Through the account of the angels, God also informs humanity about this responsibility and instills awareness of it. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Through the account of the angels, God also informs humanity about this responsibility and instills awareness of it. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q07/p90 Some maintain that reason, spiritual unveiling, and experience can establish the existence of angels and some of their attributes. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Some maintain that reason, spiritual unveiling, and experience can establish the existence of angels and some of their attributes. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q07/p91 Accounts from spiritually trained people, people aided by angels in helplessness caused by distress or illness, and people who see angels in dreams are cited as personal experiences. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Accounts from spiritually trained people, people aided by angels in helplessness caused by distress or illness, and people who see angels in dreams are cited as personal experiences. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q07/p92 One class of angels bears the Throne. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): One class of angels bears the Throne. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q07/p93 The story establishes God's comprehensive knowledge, denies the ang — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): The story establishes God's comprehensive knowledge, denies the angels knowledge except what He teaches them, and gives Adam knowledge fit for the vicegerency. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:30/q07/p94 Knowledge is central: God has comprehensive knowledge, angels know only what God teaches them, and Adam is taught what enables him to fulfill the vicegerency. — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Knowledge is central: God has comprehensive knowledge, angels know only what God teaches them, and Adam is taught what enables him to fulfill the vicegerency. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:30/q07/p95 A righteous, God-fearing, faithful human is more honored by God than the angels; God honored humanity through knowledge and intellect. — SAMARRAI-LAMASAT-HALAQAT (author): A righteous, God-fearing, faithful human is more honored by God than the angels; God honored humanity through knowledge and intellect. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-LAMASAT-HALAQAT]
- 2:30/q07/p96 The usual theological view is that angels cannot disobey or rebel. — STUDYQURAN (author): The usual theological view is that angels cannot disobey or rebel. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN]
- 2:30/q07/p97 The angels mean that they exalt God through speech and action and declare Him pure through belief in the perfections fitting the divine essence, so the verbs are not redundant. — IBNASHUR-FULL (author): The angels mean that they exalt God through speech and action and declare Him pure through belief in the perfections fitting the divine essence, so the verbs are not redundant. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:30/q07/p98 The adulterous woman becoming a luminous star and being honored by God's oath is absurd; Razi points to 81:15–16 and defers sorcery to 2:102. — RAZI-FULL (author): The adulterous woman becoming a luminous star and being honored by God's oath is absurd; Razi points to 81:15–16 and defers sorcery to 2:102. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]
- 2:30/q07/p99 The wording of 2:39 does not itself prove angels are punished in Hell; that is known from other evidence. — RAZI-FULL (author): The wording of 2:39 does not itself prove angels are punished in Hell; that is known from other evidence. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]
- 2:30/q07/p100 The specific story details that Iblīs was an angelic-tribe member are unreliable popular narratives; Ibn ʿAbbās would have known 18:50 identifies Iblīs as a jinn. — STUDYQURAN (author): The specific story details that Iblīs was an angelic-tribe member are unreliable popular narratives; Ibn ʿAbbās would have known 18:50 identifies Iblīs as a jinn. [0 notes: STUDYQURAN-]
- 2:30/q07/p101 The tale says the angels chose immediate punishment, were hung upside down in a Babylonian well until Judgment, and teach sorcery there. — RAZI-FULL (المخالفون): The tale says the angels chose immediate punishment, were hung upside down in a Babylonian well until Judgment, and teach sorcery there. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL(المخالفون)-]
- 2:30/q07/p102 Razi says the angels did not mean to alert God to something overlooked, which would be unbelief, or to censure His act; he gives other readings. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi says the angels did not mean to alert God to something overlooked, which would be unbelief, or to censure His act; he gives other readings. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]
- 2:30/q07/p103 The tale contradicts itself by having punished angels teach and promote sorcery. — RAZI-FULL (author): The tale contradicts itself by having punished angels teach and promote sorcery. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]

## 2:30/q08 What classes, tasks, and numbers of angels are described?  [لِلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةِ · reports]
- 2:30/q08/p1 Said b. Jubayr reports an episode in which Umar asks about the angels’ prayer; Gabriel says the lowest heaven’s angels prostrate, the second’s bow, and the third’s stand until the Resurrection, each reciting praises. — TAB-FULL (سعيد بن جبير): Said b. Jubayr reports an episode in which Umar asks about the angels’ prayer; Gabriel says the lowest heaven’s angels prostrate, the second’s bow, and the third’s stand until the Resurrection, each reciting praises. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(سعيد بن جبير)]
- 2:30/q08/p2 A Prophetic report says the heavens groan, rightly so, for every space the size of a foot contains an angel prostrating or bowing; Razi gives no grading. — RAZI-FULL (عليه الصلاة والسلام): A Prophetic report says the heavens groan, rightly so, for every space the size of a foot contains an angel prostrating or bowing; Razi gives no grading. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(عليه الصلاة والسلام)]
- 2:30/q08/p3 The report describes 600,000 Throne pavilions, angels in every footspace worshipping, and numberless ranks whose species, lifespans, and worship only God knows. — RAZI-FULL (author): The report describes 600,000 Throne pavilions, angels in every footspace worshipping, and numberless ranks whose species, lifespans, and worship only God knows. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p4 In a report Razi saw in an admonition book, the Prophet at the Ascension met angels moving like a marketplace; Gabriel did not know their destinations and had never seen the same angel twice. — RAZI-FULL (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): In a report Razi saw in an admonition book, the Prophet at the Ascension met angels moving like a marketplace; Gabriel did not know their destinations and had never seen the same angel twice. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 2:30/q08/p5 Razi classifies angels as bearers of the Throne, those surrounding it, and senior angels including Gabriel and Michael. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi classifies angels as bearers of the Throne, those surrounding it, and senior angels including Gabriel and Michael. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p6 Razi says reports establish Israfil and Azrael; Azrael is the Angel of Death, while the plural messengers who take souls may be a group led by him. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi says reports establish Israfil and Azrael; Azrael is the Angel of Death, while the plural messengers who take souls may be a group led by him. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p7 Gabriel conveys revelation to prophets, as in the description of the Trustworthy Spirit bringing revelation to Muhammad's heart. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel conveys revelation to prophets, as in the description of the Trustworthy Spirit bringing revelation to Muhammad's heart. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p8 Gabriel is na — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel is named before other angels; because he brings knowledge and Michael provides sustenance, spiritual nourishment makes Gabriel superior. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p9 Gabriel is named alongside God as the believers' protector, and is called the Spirit of Holiness in the address concerning Jesus. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel is named alongside God as the believers' protector, and is called the Spirit of Holiness in the address concerning Jesus. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p10 Gabriel supports God's allies and defeats enemies with a thousand marked angels. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel supports God's allies and defeats enemies with a thousand marked angels. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p11 The Qur'an praises Gabriel as a noble messenger, powerful, well-positioned near the Throne, obeyed, and trustworthy. — RAZI-FULL (author): The Qur'an praises Gabriel as a noble messenger, powerful, well-positioned near the Throne, obeyed, and trustworthy. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p12 Gabriel's mission and honor lie in mediating between God and all prophets, who are thus his community and the noblest of God's servants. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel's mission and honor lie in mediating between God and all prophets, who are thus his community and the noblest of God's servants. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p13 Gabriel's strength is shown by lifting the cities of Lot's people to heaven and overturning them. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel's strength is shown by lifting the cities of Lot's people to heaven and overturning them. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p14 Gabriel is obeyed as the angels' leader and exemplar; his trustworthiness is also affirmed by the verse about the Trustworthy Spirit. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel is obeyed as the angels' leader and exemplar; his trustworthiness is also affirmed by the verse about the Trustworthy Spirit. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p15 Ibn Abbas reports that the Prophet chose servant prophethood after Gabriel signaled humility when an angel offered kingship or servanthood. — RAZI-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas reports that the Prophet chose servant prophethood after Gabriel signaled humility when an angel offered kingship or servanthood. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q08/p16 Al-Bayhaqi's report from Ibn Abbas describes Israfil standing with lowered gaze and consulting the Preserved Tablet when commanded. — RAZI-FULL (ابن عباس): Al-Bayhaqi's report from Ibn Abbas describes Israfil standing with lowered gaze and consulting the Preserved Tablet when commanded. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q08/p17 The report assigns Gabriel winds and armies, Michael vegetation, and the Angel of Death the taking of souls; Gabriel says he feared the Hour. — RAZI-FULL (ابن عباس): The report assigns Gabriel winds and armies, Michael vegetation, and the Angel of Death the taking of souls; Gabriel says he feared the Hour. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q08/p18 Gabriel's strength let him uproot the mountains and settlements of Lot's people at once. — RAZI-FULL (author): Gabriel's strength let him uproot the mountains and settlements of Lot's people at once. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p19 The Qur'an's plural messengers taking souls can mean subordinate angels assigned to death under the Angel of Death. — RAZI-FULL (author): The Qur'an's plural messengers taking souls can mean subordinate angels assigned to death under the Angel of Death. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p20 Reports identify Israfil as the angel who blows the Trumpet, associated with the Qur'anic account of the blasts and resurrection. — RAZI-FULL (author): Reports identify Israfil as the angel who blows the Trumpet, associated with the Qur'anic account of the blasts and resurrection. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p21 Razi lists angels of Paradise, angels of Hell led by Malik and collectively called the zabaniyah, guardian angels, and recorders of deeds. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi lists angels of Paradise, angels of Hell led by Malik and collectively called the zabaniyah, guardian angels, and recorders of deeds. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p22 The homily lists tasbih by throne-bearing angels, the close and other angels, prophets, Companions, insects, animals, atoms, and all things in the heavens and earth. — RAZI-FULL (author): The homily lists tasbih by throne-bearing angels, the close and other angels, prophets, Companions, insects, animals, atoms, and all things in the heavens and earth. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p23 Gabriel tells the Prophet that angels in the first, second, and third heavens remain prostrating, standing, and bowing, praising God as Lord of dominion, might, and life. — RAZI-FULL (جبريل): Gabriel tells the Prophet that angels in the first, second, and third heavens remain prostrating, standing, and bowing, praising God as Lord of dominion, might, and life. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(جبريل)]
- 2:30/q08/p24 Sa'id ibn Jubayr reports Gabriel's account: the lowest heaven prostrates, the second bows, and the third stands until Judgment, reciting “Glory to the Lord of dominion,” “Glory to the Lord of might,” and “Glory to the — DURR-FULL (سعيد بن جبير): Sa'id ibn Jubayr reports Gabriel's account: the lowest heaven prostrates, the second bows, and the third stands until Judgment, reciting “Glory to the Lord of dominion,” “Glory to the Lord of might,” and “Glory to the Ever-Living.” [1 notes: DURR-FULL(سعيد بن جبير)]
- 2:30/q08/p25 A report scales angelic populations by successive tenfold ratios from humans, jinn, and animal kinds through the seven heavens, then describes six hundred thousand vast throne pavilions. — BURSEVI (author): A report scales angelic populations by successive tenfold ratios from humans, jinn, and animal kinds through the seven heavens, then describes six hundred thousand vast throne pavilions. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q08/p26 The same report says each span of the throne pavilions contains angels prostrating, bowing, or standing and glorifying God. — BURSEVI (author): The same report says each span of the throne pavilions contains angels prostrating, bowing, or standing and glorifying God. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q08/p27 The report says angels around the Throne are like a drop in the sea, with further countless ranks serving the Tablet and Gabriel; only God knows their kinds and worship. — BURSEVI (author): The report says angels around the Throne are like a drop in the sea, with further countless ranks serving the Tablet and Gabriel; only God knows their kinds and worship. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q08/p28 During the Ascension the Prophet saw angels continually walking past a station; Gabriel did not know where they went and had never seen one return. — BURSEVI (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): During the Ascension the Prophet saw angels continually walking past a station; Gabriel did not know where they went and had never seen one return. [1 notes: BURSEVI(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 2:30/q08/p29 The author says Gabriel, appearing before the Prophet as Diḥya al-Kalbī, did not leave the Lote Tree; he says similar manifestations occur for perfected saints. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The author says Gabriel, appearing before the Prophet as Diḥya al-Kalbī, did not leave the Lote Tree; he says similar manifestations occur for perfected saints. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p30 A report says heaven groans, for every span in it contains an angel prostrating or bowing; no grading is supplied here. — ALUSI-FULL (author): A report says heaven groans, for every span in it contains an angel prostrating or bowing; no grading is supplied here. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q08/p31 A prophetic report says heaven groans rightly because every space the size of a footstep contains an angel prostrating or bowing. — ELMALILI (author): A prophetic report says heaven groans rightly because every space the size of a footstep contains an angel prostrating or bowing. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q08/p32 The narration says all these angels are tiny beside those circling the Throne; the hosts of the Preserved Tablet and Gabriel are countless, and only God knows their kinds, lifetimes, and worship. — ELMALILI (author): The narration says all these angels are tiny beside those circling the Throne; the hosts of the Preserved Tablet and Gabriel are countless, and only God knows their kinds, lifetimes, and worship. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q08/p33 A prophetic ascent report describes battlement-like angels; Gabriel has seen them since his creation but never the same one twice, and one angel says God created 400,000 stars in its lifetime. — ELMALILI (author): A prophetic ascent report describes battlement-like angels; Gabriel has seen them since his creation but never the same one twice, and one angel says God created 400,000 stars in its lifetime. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q08/p34 Gabriel, also called Jibril and the Holy Spirit, is tasked with bringing revelation to the prophets. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Gabriel, also called Jibril and the Holy Spirit, is tasked with bringing revelation to the prophets. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p35 The Qur'an describes Gabriel as noble, powerful, honored, respected, and trustworthy. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): The Qur'an describes Gabriel as noble, powerful, honored, respected, and trustworthy. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p36 Michael is assigned to regulate natural events; the Qur'an mentions him with Gabriel and says God is an enemy to their enemies. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Michael is assigned to regulate natural events; the Qur'an mentions him with Gabriel and says God is an enemy to their enemies. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p37 Hadith reports identify Israfil as the angel assigned to blow the trumpet. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Hadith reports identify Israfil as the angel assigned to blow the trumpet. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p38 The Qur'an mentions both an angel of death and angels charged with taking people's lives. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): The Qur'an mentions both an angel of death and angels charged with taking people's lives. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p39 Although the name does not occur in sahih hadith, other Islamic literature calls the angel of death Azrail. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Although the name does not occur in sahih hadith, other Islamic literature calls the angel of death Azrail. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p40 Hell has angels who perform duties toward those suffering punishment; they are called zabani, and their leader is Malik. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Hell has angels who perform duties toward those suffering punishment; they are called zabani, and their leader is Malik. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p41 Guardian angels accompany people, watch over and protect them, wish them well, and inspire good things. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Guardian angels accompany people, watch over and protect them, wish them well, and inspire good things. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q08/p42 Recording angels remain near people and write down all their deeds. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Recording angels remain near people and write down all their deeds. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]

## 2:30/q09 Are angels free of sin and capable of disobedience?  [لِلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةِ · theology]
- 2:30/q09/p1 They are God’s messengers and never disobey Him in small or great matters. — MAWARDI-FULL (author): They are God’s messengers and never disobey Him in small or great matters. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL]
- 2:30/q09/p2 The angels’ goodness is secured by infallibility, while human wrongdoers are immersed in mercy. — QUSHAYRI (author): The angels’ goodness is secured by infallibility, while human wrongdoers are immersed in mercy. [1 notes: QUSHAYRI]
- 2:30/q09/p3 The author’s school holds angels to be accountable and free agents, while another group says they are compelled. — JISHUMI (أصحابنا): The author’s school holds angels to be accountable and free agents, while another group says they are compelled. [1 notes: JISHUMI(أصحابنا)]
- 2:30/q09/p4 The author’s school holds angels sinless, citing 66:6; another view says they are not sinless. — JISHUMI (أصحابنا): The author’s school holds angels sinless, citing 66:6; another view says they are not sinless. [1 notes: JISHUMI(أصحابنا)]
- 2:30/q09/p5 Alternatively, they knew that angels alone were infallible and other creatures would not share their character. — KASHSHAF-FULL (author): Alternatively, they knew that angels alone were infallible and other creatures would not share their character. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL]
- 2:30/q09/p6 The Hashwiyya dissent from universal angelic immunity. — RAZI-FULL (الحشوية): The Hashwiyya dissent from universal angelic immunity. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(الحشوية)]
- 2:30/q09/p7 A third objection says Iblis was a close angel, then disobeyed and disbelieved, proving angels can sin. — RAZI-FULL (المخالفون): A third objection says Iblis was a close angel, then disobeyed and disbelieved, proving angels can sin. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(المخالفون)]
- 2:30/q09/p8 They argue 21:29 threatens an angel who claims divinity and 7:206 praises angels for not being arrogant, implying capacity for evil. — RAZI-FULL (جمهور المعتزلة وكثير من الفقهاء): They argue 21:29 threatens an angel who claims divinity and 7:206 praises angels for not being arrogant, implying capacity for evil. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(جمهور المعتزلة وكثير من الفقهاء)]
- 2:30/q09/p9 They argue angels merit praise for good only if able to refrain; compelled agents or those unable to desist are not praiseworthy. — RAZI-FULL (جمهور المعتزلة وكثير من الفقهاء): They argue angels merit praise for good only if able to refrain; compelled agents or those unable to desist are not praiseworthy. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(جمهور المعتزلة وكثير من الفقهاء)]
- 2:30/q09/p10 The angels may have known of future corruption from God’s report, the Preserved Tablet, reasoned that infallibility belongs to angels, or inferred it by analogy between the two kinds of beings. — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): The angels may have known of future corruption from God’s report, the Preserved Tablet, reasoned that infallibility belongs to angels, or inferred it by analogy between the two kinds of beings. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q09/p11 Al-Shaʿrānī transmits from al-Khawwāṣ that only heavenly angels are infallible, as pure intellects without an opposing desire. — ALUSI-FULL (الخواص): Al-Shaʿrānī transmits from al-Khawwāṣ that only heavenly angels are infallible, as pure intellects without an opposing desire. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(الخواص)]
- 2:30/q09/p12 Another view says they inferred corruption from the non-infallibility of other creatures, which entails sin, conflict, and bloodshed. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): Another view says they inferred corruption from the non-infallibility of other creatures, which entails sin, conflict, and bloodshed. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q09/p13 A further view says they analogized one of the two morally accountable species to the other because both lack infallibility. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): A further view says they analogized one of the two morally accountable species to the other because both lack infallibility. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q09/p14 The clause is circumstantial, attached to the subject of “Will You place”; it states the point of difficulty: appointing one who may corrupt while the angels are infallible. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The clause is circumstantial, attached to the subject of “Will You place”; it states the point of difficulty: appointing one who may corrupt while the angels are infallible. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q09/p15 The angels seek the reason that would favor appointing the vicegerent; their question is inquiry, not astonishment or boasting that would compromise their infallibility. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The angels seek the reason that would favor appointing the vicegerent; their question is inquiry, not astonishment or boasting that would compromise their infallibility. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q09/p16 Al-Rāzī notes the Muʿtazilite view that angels can be disobedient. — STUDYQURAN (Muʿtazilites): Al-Rāzī notes the Muʿtazilite view that angels can be disobedient. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN(Muʿtazilites)]
- 2:30/q09/p17 From 2:28 onward, the passage counters disbelief and ingratitude with proofs and blessings; 2:30–35 then presents stewardship, knowledge, angelic prostration, and the Garden. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): From 2:28 onward, the passage counters disbelief and ingratitude with proofs and blessings; 2:30–35 then presents stewardship, knowledge, angelic prostration, and the Garden. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q09/p18 Razi rejects conjecture as the basis: accusing others without assurance of truth conflicts with angelic infallibility. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi rejects conjecture as the basis: accusing others without assurance of truth conflicts with angelic infallibility. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]
- 2:30/q09/p19 Al-Ālūsī rejects the claim that the interrogative is denial; the Ḥashwiyya use it to argue that angels are not infallible because they objected to God and disparaged humanity. — ALUSI-FULL (الحشوية): Al-Ālūsī rejects the claim that the interrogative is denial; the Ḥashwiyya use it to argue that angels are not infallible because they objected to God and disparaged humanity. [0 notes: ALUSI-FULL(الحشوية)-]
- 2:30/q09/p20 Al-Alusi rejects the Hashwiyya claim that the angels' comparison expresses pride and thereby harms their infallibility. — ALUSI-FULL (الحشوية): Al-Alusi rejects the Hashwiyya claim that the angels' comparison expresses pride and thereby harms their infallibility. [0 notes: ALUSI-FULL(الحشوية)-]
- 2:30/q09/p21 Al-Alusi signals flaws in the two explanations that infer the angels' knowledge from non-infallibility and analogy. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Al-Alusi signals flaws in the two explanations that infer the angels' knowledge from non-infallibility and analogy. [0 notes: ALUSI-FULL-]

## 2:30/q10 How do angels rank beside humans and prophets?  [لِلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةِ · theology]
- 2:30/q10/p1 Other positions say only the Prophet is superior to angels, or that the ranking is unknown and judgment should be suspended. — JISHUMI (قيل): Other positions say only the Prophet is superior to angels, or that the ranking is unknown and judgment should be suspended. [1 notes: JISHUMI(قيل)]
- 2:30/q10/p2 One group says believers are superior to angels; another says angels surpass believers but prophets surpass angels. — JISHUMI (جماعة): One group says believers are superior to angels; another says angels surpass believers but prophets surpass angels. [1 notes: JISHUMI(جماعة)]
- 2:30/q10/p3 Some Imāmīs hold prophets and Imams superior to angels; other views single out the Prophet or suspend judgment. — JISHUMI (جماعة من الإمامية): Some Imāmīs hold prophets and Imams superior to angels; other views single out the Prophet or suspend judgment. [1 notes: JISHUMI(جماعة من الإمامية)]
- 2:30/q10/p4 Razi reconciles the sequence: angels precede prophets in rank and nobility, but follow them in our reasoning because we establish angels through inquiry. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi reconciles the sequence: angels precede prophets in rank and nobility, but follow them in our reasoning because we establish angels through inquiry. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL+]

## 2:30/q11 Which angels were addressed?  [لِلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةِ · referent]
turns on: Whether the unqualified plural means every angel or the earthly company associated with Iblis.
- 2:30/q11/p1 The earthly angels were addressed — THALABI (author): The angels addressed here were those who had been on earth. BAGHAWI-FULL (author): The angels addressed were those who had lived on earth after God settled angels in heaven and jinn on earth; the jinn later became envious, rebellious, corrupt, and murderous. RAZI-FULL (author): A report gives successive tenfold ratios from humans and jinn through land, bird, and sea animals to earthly angels and the angels of each heaven, up to the angels of the Throne's pavilion; even all those angels are a drop beside those circling the Throne. BAYDAWI-FULL (الحكماء): In the philosophers’ account, the second class manages affairs from heaven to earth as divinely decreed; they include celestial and earthly angels. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (ابن مسعود وابن عباس): One view identifies the khalifa as Adam, succeeding earthly angels, the jinn, Iblis in earthly rule, or God; Ibn Masʿūd and Ibn ʿAbbās are cited for the last view. ALUSI-FULL (author): The second group manages affairs from heaven to earth according to the prior decree; they obey commands and include heavenly and earthly angels, whose number only God knows. ALUSI-FULL (قيل): Another view limits the address to earthly angels because the discussion concerns vicegerency on earth. ALUSI-FULL (الخواص): Al-Khawwāṣ's account says earthly angels can err and places Iblīs among them at the Emerald Mountain near the eastern equator; al-Ālūsī says this would make earthly objection conceivable, God forbid. [8 notes: THALABI; BAGHAWI-FULL; RAZI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL(الحكماء); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن مسعود وابن عباس); ALUSI-FULL(الخواص,قيل)]
- 2:30/q11/p2 All angels were addressed — IBNATIYYA-FULL (author): Angels do not know the unseen or speak ahead of God; the statement that they do not precede Him in speech praises all angels and indicates this is universal. TABRISI (author): God announces to the angels that He will create Adam as a successor on earth; the address may be to all angels or to those settled on earth after the jinn, per Ibn Abbas. RAZI-FULL (الأكثرون من الصحابة والتابعين): Most Companions and Successors say God addressed all angels: the unqualified term denotes them all, and restriction is contrary to the default. BAYDAWI-FULL (author): The unqualified address includes all angels; alternatives limit it to angels of earth or to Iblis and his angelic company, who were sent to defeat corrupting jinn that had first inhabited earth. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): The apparent reading makes the angels general; another group takes them specifically as earthly angels after the jinn or as those who fought with Iblis. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): If the angels addressed are all angels, the statement exposes Iblis's pride and reveals what God already knew; reports are cited from Ibn ʿAbbās and al-Suddī through his teachers. ELMALILI (author): These accounts exceed contemporary astronomy; subtle ether bodies cannot explain all angels, at most earthly and lowest-heaven angels unless the lowest heaven is narrowly construed, unlike theologians’ preferred view. ELMALILI (author): Most Companions and Successors understand all angels, since the wording is general and has no qualifier. [8 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL; TABRISI; RAZI-FULL(الأكثرون من الصحابة والتابعين); ABUHAYYAN-FULL; ELMALILI; BAYDAWI-FULL+; ELMALILI+]
- 2:30/q11/p3 Iblis and his angelic company were addressed — TAB-FULL (author): Al-Ṭabarī reads the address as directed to a particular angelic faction, Iblis’s companions who fought the jinn, to test them, expose their limited knowledge, and show that honor comes without bodily strength. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (قول غير مسمى): One proposed explanation is that God knew Iblis was among the angels addressed. [2 notes: TAB-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(قول غير مسمى)]
- 2:30/q11/p4 One view takes the angels addressed as all the angels. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (السدي عن أشياخه): One view takes the angels addressed as all the angels. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(السدي عن أشياخه)]
- 2:30/q11/p5 Caliphate depends on spiritual preparedness and capacity, which angels lack; the seeker must value and work to attain this great capacity. — BURSEVI (author): Caliphate depends on spiritual preparedness and capacity, which angels lack; the seeker must value and work to attain this great capacity. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q11/p6 One view restricts the angels addressed here to angels of earth, transmitted by al-Dahhak from Ibn Abbas. — ELMALILI (author): One view restricts the angels addressed here to angels of earth, transmitted by al-Dahhak from Ibn Abbas. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q11/p7 The apparent address suggests the creation troubled angels: their worship had lightened while they lived on earth, and they feared returning to heaven, where worship would burden them again. — WAHIDI-BASIT (author): The apparent address suggests the creation troubled angels: their worship had lightened while they lived on earth, and they feared returning to heaven, where worship would burden them again. [1 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT]
- 2:30/q11/p8 God’s address to the angels establishes that they are accountable beings. — JISHUMI (author): God’s address to the angels establishes that they are accountable beings. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q11/p9 Al-Ashʿari holds that God’s address is eternal, conditional on the angels’ existence and understanding; Abu al-Maʿali endorses this. — QURTUBI-FULL (أبو الحسن الأشعري وأبو المعالي): Al-Ashʿari holds that God’s address is eternal, conditional on the angels’ existence and understanding; Abu al-Maʿali endorses this. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو الحسن الأشعري وأبو المعالي)]
- 2:30/q11/p10 Al-Rāzī prefers that God addressed all the angels, rather than only those who fought alongside Iblīs. — STUDYQURAN (al-Rāzī): Al-Rāzī prefers that God addressed all the angels, rather than only those who fought alongside Iblīs. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN(al-Rāzī)+]
- 2:30/q11/p11 One view takes the address to include all angels, since the wording is general and has no qualifier restricting it. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): One view takes the address to include all angels, since the wording is general and has no qualifier restricting it. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q11/p12 Another version says an earthly woman became Venus after the angels drank wine, committed adultery and murder, worshipped an idol, and learned the Great Name enabling ascent. — RAZI-FULL (المخالفون): Another version says an earthly woman became Venus after the angels drank wine, committed adultery and murder, worshipped an idol, and learned the Great Name enabling ascent. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL(المخالفون)-]

## 2:30/q12 Why did God announce the successor to the angels?  [إِنِّى جَاعِلٌۭ · interpretation]
- 2:30/q12/p1 God announced Adam as vicegerent before creating him. — TUSTARI (author): God announced Adam as vicegerent before creating him. [1 notes: TUSTARI]
- 2:30/q12/p2 After mentioning the creation of people and others, the passage links their creation to the time God told the angels He would place a successor on earth. — ZAJJAJ (author): After mentioning the creation of people and others, the passage links their creation to the time God told the angels He would place a successor on earth. [1 notes: ZAJJAJ]
- 2:30/q12/p3 Al-Suddi says God consulted the angels about creating Adam; Qatadah is also reported to hold this account. — IBNABIHATIM (السدي): Al-Suddi says God consulted the angels about creating Adam; Qatadah is also reported to hold this account. [1 notes: IBNABIHATIM(السدي)]
- 2:30/q12/p4 Ibn Abbas says God brought Adam out of the Garden before he had lived in it, and cites the announcement of an earthly khalifa. — IBNABIHATIM (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says God brought Adam out of the Garden before he had lived in it, and cites the announcement of an earthly khalifa. [1 notes: IBNABIHATIM(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q12/p5 Hasan says God told the angels He wou — IBNABIHATIM (الحسن): Hasan says God told the angels He would act; they offered their opinion based on knowledge He had given them, while He withheld other knowledge. [1 notes: IBNABIHATIM(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q12/p6 God addressed the angels to expose their reliance on their own actions, worship, and purification, not to seek their counsel; He then returned them to their proper station by ordering prostration to Adam. — SULAMI (بعضهم): God addressed the angels to expose their reliance on their own actions, worship, and purification, not to seek their counsel; He then returned them to their proper station by ordering prostration to Adam. [1 notes: SULAMI(بعضهم)]
- 2:30/q12/p7 Al-Suddi says God told the angels that the successor would have descendants who corrupt, envy, and kill one another, explaining how they knew the unseen. — THALABI (السدي): Al-Suddi says God told the angels that the successor would have descendants who corrupt, envy, and kill one another, explaining how they knew the unseen. [1 notes: THALABI(السدي)]
- 2:30/q12/p8 God gave Iblis dominion over earth, the lowest heaven, and the gardens’ storehouses; he worshipped across earth, heaven, and paradise, then grew proud and resented removal when a successor was announced. — THALABI (المفسرون): God gave Iblis dominion over earth, the lowest heaven, and the gardens’ storehouses; he worshipped across earth, heaven, and paradise, then grew proud and resented removal when a successor was announced. [1 notes: THALABI(المفسرون)]
- 2:30/q12/p9 God's announcement about Adam disclosed a secret and divided the angels' conjectures, despite their wonder at the artistry and wisdom of his form. — QUSHAYRI (author): God's announcement about Adam disclosed a secret and divided the angels' conjectures, despite their wonder at the artistry and wisdom of his form. [1 notes: QUSHAYRI]
- 2:30/q12/p10 Among created things, Adam alone receives this explicit announcement; its wording would resemble consultation from a creature, but here it honors and singles him out. — QUSHAYRI (author): Among created things, Adam alone receives this explicit announcement; its wording would resemble consultation from a creature, but here it honors and singles him out. [1 notes: QUSHAYRI]
- 2:30/q12/p11 God’s informing the angels is glad tidings about Adam’s honored status, following the practice of announcing prophets before their creation and mission; it is not consultation, which is impossible for the omniscient God. — WAHIDI-BASIT (author): God’s informing the angels is glad tidings about Adam’s honored status, following the practice of announcing prophets before their creation and mission; it is not consultation, which is impossible for the omniscient God. [1 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT]
- 2:30/q12/p12 The missing divine announcement is inferred from the angels' answer: the text omits it for concision, as al-Shanfara's poetry also omits an understood phrase. — WAHIDI-BASIT (author): The missing divine announcement is inferred from the angels' answer: the text omits it for concision, as al-Shanfara's poetry also omits an understood phrase. [1 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT]
- 2:30/q12/p13 Another explanation is that God taught servants to consult trusted, sincere advisers before acting, despite needing no consultation Himself. — KASHSHAF-FULL (قيل): Another explanation is that God taught servants to consult trusted, sincere advisers before acting, despite needing no consultation Himself. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q12/p14 They also relate the passage to the prior statement that God created everything on earth for humanity, with creation beginning when He addressed the angels. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (الجمهور): They also relate the passage to the prior statement that God created everything on earth for humanity, with creation beginning when He addressed the angels. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الجمهور)]
- 2:30/q12/p15 God’s announcement that He would place a successor on earth tests the angels, brings about their response, and instructs them through Adam’s teaching and honor. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (author): God’s announcement that He would place a successor on earth tests the angels, brings about their response, and instructs them through Adam’s teaching and honor. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p16 Al-Hasan says God announced Adam’s creation to test the angels’ obedience. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (الحسن): Al-Hasan says God announced Adam’s creation to test the angels’ obedience. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q12/p17 God told the angels because He knew they would ask about the secret, and it benefited them to hear the answer. — RAZI-FULL (author): God told the angels because He knew they would ask about the secret, and it benefited them to hear the answer. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p18 The announcement also teaches people to consult one another. — RAZI-FULL (author): The announcement also teaches people to consult one another. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p19 The announcement of Adam’s creation and honor, including God’s command that the angels prostrate, is a third favor extending to all his descendants. — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): The announcement of Adam’s creation and honor, including God’s command that the angels prostrate, is a third favor extending to all his descendants. [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p20 Al-Ḥasan says the announcement tests the angels' obedience. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (الحسن): Al-Ḥasan says the announcement tests the angels' obedience. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q12/p21 The announcement could teach that Adam was created to inhabit earth although his creation began in heaven, and model consulting learned people despite already knowing the answer. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): The announcement could teach that Adam was created to inhabit earth although his creation began in heaven, and model consulting learned people despite already knowing the answer. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p22 Ibn Zayd says the announcement reassured angels who feared the Fire and believed no creatures existed besides themselves; God said it was for those who disobey Him. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (ابن زيد): Ibn Zayd says the announcement reassured angels who feared the Fire and believed no creatures existed besides themselves; God said it was for those who disobey Him. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن زيد)]
- 2:30/q12/p23 A report attributed to the Prophet says earth was spread from Mecca, angels first circled the House, and “earth” in the khalifa announcement meant Mecca. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (عبد الرحمن بن سابط): A report attributed to the Prophet says earth was spread from Mecca, angels first circled the House, and “earth” in the khalifa announcement meant Mecca. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(عبد الرحمن بن سابط)]
- 2:30/q12/p24 Al-Suddi says God consulted the angels about creating Adam; Ibn Kathir says this wording is loose unless it means that God informed them, and prefers al-Hasan and Qatada's wording. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (السدي): Al-Suddi says God consulted the angels about creating Adam; Ibn Kathir says this wording is loose unless it means that God informed them, and prefers al-Hasan and Qatada's wording. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(السدي)]
- 2:30/q12/p25 The first purpose of telling the angels is to teach consultation with trustworthy advisers before acting, despite God's complete knowledge and wisdom. — BURSEVI (author): The first purpose of telling the angels is to teach consultation with trustworthy advisers before acting, despite God's complete knowledge and wisdom. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q12/p26 The cited Mathnawī verses say consultation brings insight and wisdom, as minds assist one another. — BURSEVI (المثنوي): The cited Mathnawī verses say consultation brings insight and wisdom, as minds assist one another. [1 notes: BURSEVI(المثنوي)]
- 2:30/q12/p27 Bursevī cites the Prophet's instruction to consult, since the person consulted is entrusted with confidence. — BURSEVI (النبي): Bursevī cites the Prophet's instruction to consult, since the person consulted is entrusted with confidence. [1 notes: BURSEVI(النبي)]
- 2:30/q12/p28 Announcing the plan to the angels may teach consultation in a matter resembling it, honor the appointed creature, or display his merit. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Announcing the plan to the angels may teach consultation in a matter resembling it, honor the appointed creature, or display his merit. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q12/p29 The Shaykh al-Akbar dates this announcement to Virgo after 17,000 years of the world's age; he gives 8,000 years for the endless afterlife and 71,000 conventional years for the bounded natural world. — ALUSI-FULL (الشيخ الأكبر): The Shaykh al-Akbar dates this announcement to Virgo after 17,000 years of the world's age; he gives 8,000 years for the endless afterlife and 71,000 conventional years for the bounded natural world. [1 notes: ELMALILI]

[1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR+]

[1 notes: THALABI(المفسرون)]
- 2:30/q12/p33 The primary referent is Adam, and more generally every prophet God appoints to cultivate the earth, govern people, perfect their souls, and execute His command; this appointment answers human inability to receive divine command without an — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): The primary referent is Adam, and more generally every prophet God appoints to cultivate the earth, govern people, perfect their souls, and execute His command; this appointment answers human inability to receive divine command without an intermediary, not any need in God for a deputy. ## 2:30/q13 What does jāʿil mean here?  [جَاعِلٌۭ · meaning]
turns on: Whether jaʿala means creation or appointment with a second object.
- 2:30/q13/p1 Jāʿil means creating — TAB-FULL (author): Abu Rawq calls every ja'ala in the Quran creating. MAWARDI-FULL (author): One meaning of jāʿil is “creator.” BAGHAWI-FULL (author): Ja'il means creator. TABRISI (author): جعل, خلق, فعل, and إحداث are related, but جعل can assign a state without creating; فعل and إحداث mean bringing into existence. BURSEVI (التأويلات النجمية): The Najmiyya interpretation says jaʿl is broader than creation: it includes creating Adam and additionally endowing him with the special attribute of caliphate, as with David. ELMALILI (author): The creation of the human is specified later in the verses about making a human from clay; the announcement of jaʿil follows the conception of khalq, distinguishing the determination of essence from attributes. [6 notes: TAB-FULL(أبو روق); MAWARDI-FULL; BAGHAWI-FULL; TABRISI; BURSEVI(التأويلات النجمية); ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q13/p2 Jāʿil means making or appointing — TAB-FULL (author): Hasan and Qatada take ja'il as making. IBNATIYYA-FULL (الحسن): Al-Hasan and Qatada take jaʿil in its sense of “doing” or “making.” BURSEVI (author): Jāʿil means making or appointing; the khalīfa is Adam, succeeding the jinn on earth, where the prior corruption and oppression occurred. [3 notes: TAB-FULL(الحسن وقتادة); IBNATIYYA-FULL(الحسن); BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q13/p3 Jaʿl changes something from its prior state without bringing it into existence, unlike fiʿl and iḥdāth. — JISHUMI (author): Jaʿl changes something from its prior state without bringing it into existence, unlike fiʿl and iḥdāth. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q13/p4 Al-Zamakhsharī takes the hamza in أتجعل as astonishment. — SAMIN-DURR (الزمخشري): Al-Zamakhsharī takes the hamza in أتجعل as astonishment. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR(الزمخشري)]
- 2:30/q13/p5 Tabari prefers appointing a replacement on earth. — TAB-FULL (author): Tabari prefers appointing a replacement on earth. [1 notes: TAB-FULL+]
- 2:30/q13/p6 Al-Zajjāj says the hamza affirms making here and cites Jarīr’s ألستم as evidence for the form’s affirmative force. — ZAJJAJ (author): Al-Zajjāj says the hamza affirms making here and cites Jarīr’s ألستم as evidence for the form’s affirmative force. [1 notes: ZAJJAJ]
- 2:30/q13/p7 Hasan glosses “I am appointing” as “I am doing.” — IBNABIHATIM (الحسن): Hasan glosses “I am appointing” as “I am doing.” [1 notes: IBNABIHATIM(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q13/p8 Alternatively, making is giving something a particular state, while origination is bringing it into existence after nonexistence. — MAWARDI-FULL (author): Alternatively, making is giving something a particular state, while origination is bringing it into existence after nonexistence. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL]
- 2:30/q13/p9 Another estimate ties this to the beginning of creation: God began creating people when He said this. — JISHUMI (author): Another estimate ties this to the beginning of creation: God began creating people when He said this. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q13/p10 God honors Adam's descendants by mentioning them to the highest assembly before creating them; the verse tells Muhammad to recall this and recount it to his people. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): God honors Adam's descendants by mentioning them to the highest assembly before creating them; the verse tells Muhammad to recall this and recount it to his people. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL]
- 2:30/q13/p11 Al-Hasan glosses “I am placing” as “I am making.” — DURR-FULL (الحسن): Al-Hasan glosses “I am placing” as “I am making.” [1 notes: DURR-FULL(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q13/p12 Ibn Abbas says God brought Adam out of Paradise before creating him, then cites this verse. — DURR-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says God brought Adam out of Paradise before creating him, then cites this verse. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q13/p13 Mention of creation for humanity prepared listeners for the account of humanity's origin, which displays God's power, favor in creating and privileging humans, and appointing them as stewards. — IBNASHUR-FULL (author): Mention of creation for humanity prepared listeners for the account of humanity's origin, which displays God's power, favor in creating and privileging humans, and appointing them as stewards. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:30/q13/p14 Humanity's first creation is the striking beginning of the life that underlies God's creation of earthly things for people, making this transition apt and independently momentous. — IBNASHUR-FULL (author): Humanity's first creation is the striking beginning of the life that underlies God's creation of earthly things for people, making this transition apt and independently momentous. [1 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL]
- 2:30/q13/p15 Razi replies that Mu'tazilites deem divine reward obligatory, making God's omission impossible, yet praise Him for granting it; inability to omit does not preclude praise. — RAZI-FULL (author): Razi replies that Mu'tazilites deem divine reward obligatory, making God's omission impossible, yet praise Him for granting it; inability to omit does not preclude praise. [0 notes: RAZI-FULL-]

## 2:30/q14 How is jāʿil construed?  [جَاعِلٌۭ · grammar]
- 2:30/q14/p1 The yāʾ in «إني» is accusative and «جاعل» is the predicate of «إن»; the original «إنني» loses a nūn where two meet. — NAHHAS (author): The yāʾ in «إني» is accusative and «جاعل» is the predicate of «إن»; the original «إنني» loses a nūn where two meet. [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:30/q14/p2 «الأرض» is genitive after «في», while «خليفة» is accusative after «جاعل». — NAHHAS (author): «الأرض» is genitive after «في», while «خليفة» is accusative after «جاعل». [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:30/q14/p3 «خليفة» is the object of «جاعل»; its tanwīn cannot be omitted with the intervening phrase, though it could if the object immediately followed. — NAHHAS (author): «خليفة» is the object of «جاعل»; its tanwīn cannot be omitted with the intervening phrase, though it could if the object immediately followed. [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:30/q14/p4 «أتجعل» is a future verb. — NAHHAS (author): «أتجعل» is a future verb. [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:30/q14/p5 «من يفسد» is accusative as the first object of «تجعل»; «فيها» supplies the second object's position on the singular wording. — NAHHAS (author): «من يفسد» is accusative as the first object of «تجعل»; «فيها» supplies the second object's position on the singular wording. [1 notes: NAHHAS]
- 2:30/q14/p6 جاعل is from a verb taking two objects; ‘in the earth’ and ‘a successor’ fill the subject-predicate object positions, meaning to make someone a successor there. — KASHSHAF-FULL (author): جاعل is from a verb taking two objects; ‘in the earth’ and ‘a successor’ fill the subject-predicate object positions, meaning to make someone a successor there. [1 notes: KASHSHAF-FULL]
- 2:30/q14/p7 Abu Rawq, as reported by al-Tabari, takes jaʿil to mean “creator”; its taking one object supports the sense. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (أبو روق): Abu Rawq, as reported by al-Tabari, takes jaʿil to mean “creator”; its taking one object supports the sense. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أبو روق)]
- 2:30/q14/p8 The circumstantial phrase is governed by أتجعل فيها من يفسد; باء in بحمدك attaches to نسبح and لام in لك to نقدس. — TABRISI (author): The circumstantial phrase is governed by أتجعل فيها من يفسد; باء in بحمدك attaches to نسبح and لام in لك to نقدس. [1 notes: TABRISI]
- 2:30/q14/p9 Al-Tabari, citing Abu Rawq, says jaʿil here means “creator”; its single object supports this. — QURTUBI-FULL (الطبري): Al-Tabari, citing Abu Rawq, says jaʿil here means “creator”; its single object supports this. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الطبري)]
- 2:30/q14/p10 جاعل from جعل takes two objects, here في الأرض and خليفة, because it is future and depends on a subject; it can also mean ‘creator.’ — BAYDAWI-FULL (author): جاعل from جعل takes two objects, here في الأرض and خليفة, because it is future and depends on a subject; it can also mean ‘creator.’ [1 notes: BAYDAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q14/p11 جاعل means making or appointing, from a verb taking two objects: خليفة and في الأرض. — NASAFI-FULL (author): جاعل means making or appointing, from a verb taking two objects: خليفة and في الأرض. [1 notes: NASAFI-FULL]
- 2:30/q14/p12 Jāʿil is an active participle with future force; if it governs its object, that is better than annexation for Sibawayh, while al-Kisāʾī considers both equal and Abū Ḥayyān prefers annexation. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Jāʿil is an active participle with future force; if it governs its object, that is better than annexation for Sibawayh, while al-Kisāʾī considers both equal and Abū Ḥayyān prefers annexation. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q14/p13 إني جاعل في الأرض خليفة is the quoted content and direct object of قال; إن is closed because it follows قال without a meaning of supposition. — SAMIN-DURR (author): إني جاعل في الأرض خليفة is the quoted content and direct object of قال; إن is closed because it follows قال without a meaning of supposition. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR]
- 2:30/q14/p14 If جاعل means خالق, خليفة is its object and في الأرض either attaches to جاعل or is an omitted phrase circumstantially modifying the following indefinite noun. — SAMIN-DURR (author): If جاعل means خالق, خليفة is its object and في الأرض either attaches to جاعل or is an omitted phrase circumstantially modifying the following indefinite noun. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR]
- 2:30/q14/p15 Al-Zamakhshari takes جاعل to mean صير, making خليفة the first object and في الأرض the fronted second object. — SAMIN-DURR (الزمخشري): Al-Zamakhshari takes جاعل to mean صير, making خليفة the first object and في الأرض the fronted second object. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR(الزمخشري)]
- 2:30/q14/p16 خليفة is accusative as the object of جاعل; active participles govern like their verbs and may be annexed to their object for lightness if nothing intervenes. — SAMIN-DURR (author): خليفة is accusative as the object of جاعل; active participles govern like their verbs and may be annexed to their object for lightness if nothing intervenes. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR]
- 2:30/q14/p17 If تجعل means create, the first فيها is attached to it and من يفسد is its object. — SAMIN-DURR (author): If تجعل means create, the first فيها is attached to it and من يفسد is its object. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR]
- 2:30/q14/p18 If تجعل means make into, the first فيها is its second object, advanced before من يفسد. — SAMIN-DURR (author): If تجعل means make into, the first فيها is its second object, advanced before من يفسد. [1 notes: SAMIN-DURR]
- 2:30/q14/p19 A statement may be emphatically confirmed even when its audience has no doubt, as in God's declaration إني جاعل, strengthened by inna and the active participle. — BURHAN (author): A statement may be emphatically confirmed even when its audience has no doubt, as in God's declaration إني جاعل, strengthened by inna and the active participle. [1 notes: BURHAN]
- 2:30/q14/p20 Jāʿil can mean “making into,” taking khalīfa and “in the earth” as two objects; or “creating,” with one object and “in the earth” modifying khalīfa. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Jāʿil can mean “making into,” taking khalīfa and “in the earth” as two objects; or “creating,” with one object and “in the earth” modifying khalīfa. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q14/p21 The word jaʿil may mean create: when jaʿl takes one object it means creation, and here it is broader and more general than khalq while allowing both senses. — ELMALILI (author): The word jaʿil may mean create: when jaʿl takes one object it means creation, and here it is broader and more general than khalq while allowing both senses. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q14/p22 Although the appointment has not yet occurred, جاعل presents it as settled and inevitable through the active participle. — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Although the appointment has not yet occurred, جاعل presents it as settled and inevitable through the active participle. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR]

## 2:30/q15 What does “the earth” denote here?  [ٱلْأَرْضِ · referent]
turns on: Whether the unqualified earth means the whole earth or Mecca in the transmitted report.
- 2:30/q15/p1 The earth here is Mecca — TAB-FULL (author): A report places the earth's spreading from Mecca, the House and prophets' graves there. MAWARDI-FULL (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): Ibn Sābiṭ reports the Prophet said that the earth was spread out from Mecca. MAWARDI-FULL (author): The text says this is why Mecca was called Umm al-Qurā, and locates the graves of Noah, Hūd, Ṣāliḥ, and Shuʿayb between Zamzam, the Corner, and the Station. IBNATIYYA-FULL (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): A report from the Prophet says “the earth” here means Mecca, from beneath which earth was spread; it was where prophets’ peoples were destroyed, and Noah’s, Hud’s, and Salih’s graves lie between the Station and the Corner. TABRISI (النبي): The Prophet is cited as saying that the earth was spread out from Mecca, which is why it is called the Mother of Towns. RAZI-FULL (author): Razi judges the whole-earth interpretation closer to the apparent wording than the Mecca report. QURTUBI-FULL (رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم): Ibn Sabit reports that the earth was spread from Mecca, called Umm al-Qura, and that Noah, Hud, Salih, and Shuʿayb are buried between Zamzam and the Corner and Station. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (الجمهور): The apparent sense of fī al-arḍ is the whole earth, as the majority hold; another view identifies the land as Mecca. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (ابن سابط): Ibn Sābiṭ transmits the Mecca interpretation as a report traced to the Prophet; Abū Ḥayyān says it should be followed if authentic. ALUSI-FULL (الجمهور): The majority take “the earth” to mean the whole earth; another view identifies it as Mecca, based on a report attributed to the Prophet that the author considers probably unsound. TABRISI (author): A view takes الأرض as Mecca, citing its being the first land spread and a report about prophets' graves there; the author prefers the apparent, known earth. IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): Ibn Kathir grades that report mursal with a weak chain and an inserted interpretation that “earth” means Mecca; he says the apparent sense is the wider earth. [10 notes: TAB-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); MAWARDI-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); IBNATIYYA-FULL(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم); TABRISI(النبي); QURTUBI-FULL(رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم); ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن سابط,الجمهور); ALUSI-FULL(الجمهور); RAZI-FULL+; TABRISI-; IBNKATHIR-FULL-]
- 2:30/q15/p2 The earth means the whole earth — RAZI-FULL (author): The earth most naturally means all the earth from east to west. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL+]

## 2:30/q16 Why is the earth specified for the successor?  [فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ · interpretation]
- 2:30/q16/p1 Another account says the passage gathers evidence for divine unity from earth and heaven, then follows with the beginning of creation and Adam. — JISHUMI (author): Another account says the passage gathers evidence for divine unity from earth and heaven, then follows with the beginning of creation and Adam. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q16/p2 Another explanation says the announcement taught Adam he was created for earthly life, not Paradise; the angels sought the wisdom of that arrangement. — TABRISI (author): Another explanation says the announcement taught Adam he was created for earthly life, not Paradise; the angels sought the wisdom of that arrangement. [1 notes: TABRISI]
- 2:30/q16/p3 The four terms form earthly and heavenly pairs, with the soul positioned between them; corruption and sanctification are the far ends, and the middle terms bridge earth and heaven. — BURHAN (author): The four terms form earthly and heavenly pairs, with the soul positioned between them; corruption and sanctification are the far ends, and the middle terms bridge earth and heaven. [1 notes: BURHAN]
- 2:30/q16/p4 Mentioning earth signals that earth angels challenged Adam; heavenly angels administer the upper world, while earth angels envied the caliphate and their own dominion. — BURSEVI (author): Mentioning earth signals that earth angels challenged Adam; heavenly angels administer the upper world, while earth angels envied the caliphate and their own dominion. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q16/p5 Earth is specified because it is the realm of change and transformation, where the effects of all divine names sought by God become manifest, unlike the higher realm. — ALUSI-FULL (author): Earth is specified because it is the realm of change and transformation, where the effects of all divine names sought by God become manifest, unlike the higher realm. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q16/p6 They reason that the successor's purpose is to glorify and sanctify God, but earthly life draws the human toward evil; they claim angels already fulfill that purpose and ask why appoint a human. — TABATABAI (author): They reason that the successor's purpose is to glorify and sanctify God, but earthly life draws the human toward evil; they claim angels already fulfill that purpose and ask why appoint a human. [1 notes: TABATABAI]

## 2:30/q17 What does khalīfa mean and how is it formed?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · meaning]
- 2:30/q17/p1 Khalīfa denotes one who succeeds another — TAB-FULL (author): Khalifa denotes succeeding another, with 10:14 and rulers as examples. TAB-FULL (author): Khalifa denotes succeeding another, with 10:14 and rulers as examples. TAB-FULL (author): Khalifa denotes succeeding another, with 10:14 and rulers as examples. NAHHAS (author): «خليفة» may be active: one who succeeds the angels or other predecessors in the earth, as also related. NAHHAS (author): Alternatively «خليفة» may be passive, one who is succeeded. THALABI (author): Khalifa means a replacement for those before, succeeding them after they depart. MAWARDI-FULL (author): With fatḥa, khalaf denotes righteous successors and with sukūn, wicked successors; the Qur’an and a hadith illustrate the distinction. JISHUMI (author): Khalaf with fatḥa on the lām denotes righteous successors, while sukūn denotes bad successors, as illustrated in 7:169. IBNATIYYA-FULL (author): Khalīfa means one who succeeds another. IBNJAWZI-ZAD (ابن الأنباري): Khalīfa means one who succeeds another; Ibn al-Anbārī says its original form was khalīf, with the feminine ending added to intensify praise, like ʿallāma and nasāba. QURTUBI-FULL (author): Khalifa as an active noun is a successor to angels or other earlier beings; as a passive noun, it means one made a successor. QURTUBI-FULL (author): Khalaf with a vowel refers to righteous successors, while the still form refers to wicked ones. BAYDAWI-FULL (author): خليفة is one who succeeds or represents another, with its feminine ending marking intensification. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (الجمهور): The majority read khalīfa with fāʾ; it can mean an active successor or one replaced, and in the active sense one entrusted to act in another's place. SAMIN-DURR (author): خليفة can mean an active successor, succeeding the people addressed or the jinn who preceded them; this is stronger because its feminine suffix is regular for the active sense. SAMIN-DURR (author): Alternatively, خليفة means one succeeded by each later generation; the feminine suffix is not regular for this passive sense unless the word has become noun-like, as in النطيحة and الذبيحة. IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): Khalifa here means people succeeding one another generation after generation, as in the linked verses about successors on earth. ALUSI-FULL (author): A khalīfa succeeds another and acts in their place; its feminine ending is intensive, so the term can designate a man. ELMALILI (author): God's appointing His chosen servants as successors is a form of deputation bestowed purely to honor them. KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Khilāfa means succeeding another, representing that person, or exercising that person's authority. STUDYQURAN (author): In other passages khalīfah means successor, as in 7:69 for successors after Noah’s people; related references include 7:74, 10:14, 10:73, 27:62, and 35:39. NASAFI-FULL (author): Khalīfa means a successor, with a feminine hāʾ for emphasis. [22 notes: TAB-FULL; NAHHAS; THALABI; MAWARDI-FULL; JISHUMI; IBNATIYYA-FULL; IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن الأنباري); QURTUBI-FULL; BAYDAWI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الجمهور); SAMIN-DURR; IBNKATHIR-FULL; ALUSI-FULL; ELMALILI; KURANYOLU-TEFSIR; STUDYQURAN; NASAFI-FULL; SAMIN-DURR+]
- 2:30/q17/p2 The feminine ending of khalīfa intensifies or nominalizes the form — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Khalīfa is on faʿīla, which can be an intensive active form like al-ʿalīm or a passive form like al-naṭīḥa; its tāʾ can intensify. ELMALILI (author): Khalifa is an intensive nominal adjective from khalif, on the faʿīl pattern with the meaning of fāʿil; French représentant conveys the sense, with khalāʾif and khulafāʾ as plurals and khilāfa as the verbal noun. [2 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL; ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q17/p3 God formed the vicegerent according to prior knowledge, composed his form, clothed him in signs of divine attributes so he might know God, and held his breaths in reserve until their manifestation. — SULAMI (الواسطي): God formed the vicegerent according to prior knowledge, composed his form, clothed him in signs of divine attributes so he might know God, and held his breaths in reserve until their manifestation. [1 notes: SULAMI(الواسطي)]
- 2:30/q17/p4 خليفة may be singular or plural and apply to male or female. — RAZI-FULL (author): خليفة may be singular or plural and apply to male or female. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p5 There is no need to supply an omitted khalifa, since wording without ellipsis is better; the active participle conveys a settled state rather than gradual renewal. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): There is no need to supply an omitted khalifa, since wording without ellipsis is better; the active participle conveys a settled state rather than gradual renewal. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p6 Whether jaʿala means create or appoint, Adam became khalifa only once, so the act was not repeated. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Whether jaʿala means create or appoint, Adam became khalifa only once, so the act was not repeated. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p7 Under the first three readings, angels' prior knowledge may come from God, the Preserved Tablet, observation of other created beings, analogy with earlier earthly inhabitants, or inference from the word khalifa and its association with injustice. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Under the first three readings, angels' prior knowledge may come from God, the Preserved Tablet, observation of other created beings, analogy with earlier earthly inhabitants, or inference from the word khalifa and its association with injustice. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p8 Al-Hasan and Qatada, in Ibn Jarir's report, explain God's statement as informing the angels that He was going to create a khalifa. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (الحسن وقتادة): Al-Hasan and Qatada, in Ibn Jarir's report, explain God's statement as informing the angels that He was going to create a khalifa. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(الحسن وقتادة)]
