- 2:30/q17/p9 Al-Baḥr prefers “creating,” given the contrast; the transformative sense would require an explicit or implied khalīfa. — ALUSI-FULL (البحر): Al-Baḥr prefers “creating,” given the contrast; the transformative sense would require an explicit or implied khalīfa. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(البحر)+]
- 2:30/q17/p10 On the creation reading, “in the earth” modifies khalīfa and is fronted to create anticipation; the active participle works with future sense and dependence on a subject. — ALUSI-FULL (author): On the creation reading, “in the earth” modifies khalīfa and is fronted to create anticipation; the active participle works with future sense and dependence on a subject. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p11 One view takes the khalīfa as Adam and his descendants, supported by the angels' wording; their exchange would then disclose Adam's superiority as the origin of those who follow him. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): One view takes the khalīfa as Adam and his descendants, supported by the angels' wording; their exchange would then disclose Adam's superiority as the origin of those who follow him. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q17/p12 God means that He knows the wisdom in appointing the vicegerent, of which the angels know nothing. — ALUSI-FULL (author): God means that He knows the wisdom in appointing the vicegerent, of which the angels know nothing. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q17/p13 God's making a vicegerent means granting him some divine powers and attributes so that he may exercise delegated authority over creation in God's name. — ELMALILI (author): God's making a vicegerent means granting him some divine powers and attributes so that he may exercise delegated authority over creation in God's name. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q17/p14 The angels may have inferred the possibility of corruption from the title khalifa: vicegerency entails reform and control of those under the vicegerent, but also the possibility of wrongdoing. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The angels may have inferred the possibility of corruption from the title khalifa: vicegerency entails reform and control of those under the vicegerent, but also the possibility of wrongdoing. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]

## 2:30/q18 Who is the khalīfa?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · referent]
turns on: Whether succession is to God, earlier inhabitants, or earlier generations, and whether Adam or humanity is intended.
- 2:30/q18/p1 The successor is humanity across generations — TAB-FULL (ابن إسحاق): Ibn Ishaq explains khalifa as a nonangelic inhabitant who settles and cultivates the earth; Adam’s creation was a test, and God had disclosed that his descendants would sin and shed blood. MAWARDI-FULL (ابن مسعود): Ibn Masʿūd says the khalīfa is God’s deputy in judging among creation: Adam and those of his descendants who take his place. TABRISI (author): Another view calls Adam and his descendants successors to the angels who had inhabited earth before them. RAZI-FULL (الحسن): Al-Hasan says the successor is Adam's descendants, who follow one another, supported by the verse about successors on earth. BAYDAWI-FULL (author): Other interpretations take خليفة as a successor to earth’s previous inhabitants, or as Adam together with his descendants succeeding predecessors or one another; the singular may stand for his descendants, ‘one who succeeds you,’ or a collective succession. NASAFI-FULL (author): The khalifa may mean a successor to the angels, who had inhabited earth before Adam and his descendants. SAMIN-DURR (author): The singular خليفة can intend Adam and his descendants collectively, using the ancestor as with tribal names Mudar and Rabi'a; another view understands خليفة as a generic. BIQAI-FULL (author): Adam’s succession extends across the earth through himself and his descendants; the singular khalīfa suits the completeness of this role, though it can also be generic. BIQAI-FULL (author): A khalīfa is one who carries out the predecessor’s work according to that deputy’s rank; humans succeed one another, while Adam is God’s deputy in the realm of dominion. BURSEVI (author): God’s announcement of Adam’s caliphate displays his honor to the angels, so they should revere him rather than reject the human traits shown by him and his descendants. [10 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن إسحاق); MAWARDI-FULL(ابن مسعود); TABRISI; RAZI-FULL(الحسن); BAYDAWI-FULL; NASAFI-FULL; SAMIN-DURR; BIQAI-FULL; BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p2 Adam succeeds the earthly angels — IBNABIHATIM (محمد بن إسحاق): Ibn Ishaq says the khalifa is a being created apart from the angels to dwell in and cultivate the earth. THALABI (author): God knows the benefit in appointing a successor on earth, so the angels should not object to His judgment and governance. JISHUMI (author): The author prefers the interrogative: it asks about the angels’ state relative to the appointment, not whether God will appoint a successor. BAGHAWI-FULL (author): The announced khalifa is a replacement for the angels, whom God will raise up to Him; they disliked this because they had been the least worshipful angels. TABRISI (author): إني جاعل في الأرض خليفة is the object clause of قال; the angels' speech is the object clause of قالوا. IBNJAWZI-ZAD (ابن زيد): Ibn Zayd says the angels feared after God created fire and told them it was for those who disobeyed; they feared they might disobey, so God announced the earthly khalīfa. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Both meanings are possible, but creation is stronger: if the first jaʿala meant appointment, the angels' reply would have explicitly called its object a khalifa. IBNKATHIR-FULL (محمد بن إسحاق): Ibn Ishaq understands the khalifa as an inhabitant and cultivator who will live on and populate earth, a successor unlike the angels. IBNKATHIR-FULL (عبد الرحمن بن زيد بن أسلم): God announced that He would create beings and appoint a successor on an otherwise empty earth where only angels existed. ELMALILI (author): The answer says angels do not know the wisdom, motives, and fitness of caliphate; it also implies angelic qualities alone are insufficient and that requesting office is improper. [10 notes: IBNABIHATIM(محمد بن إسحاق); THALABI; BAGHAWI-FULL; TABRISI; IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن زيد); IBNKATHIR-FULL(عبد الرحمن بن زيد بن أسلم,محمد بن إسحاق); ELMALILI; ABUHAYYAN-FULL+; JISHUMI+]
- 2:30/q18/p3 The successor is Adam — THALABI (بعض الحكماء): A sage argues that Adam was destined to leave paradise before entering it: the earthly successor was announced first, and Adam’s later departure after sin shows divine decree. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): The prophets are God's successors on earth, and Adam alone is named because he is the forefather of all successors, as tribal names can stand for the whole tribe. BURSEVI (author): Adam is called created from clay in reference to his body, but made a caliph in reference to his spirit; this marks his distinction and dignity. [3 notes: THALABI(بعض الحكماء); ABUHAYYAN-FULL; BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p4 Adam is God's appointed deputy — NASAFI-FULL (author): Alternatively Adam is God’s vicegerent on earth, as every prophet is; the author cites God’s address to David in 38:26. ALUSI-FULL (السادة الصوفية): They say the angel appeared fully in the Muhammadan Reality, as indicated by the revelation of a spirit from God's command; hence Muhammad is creation's supreme and true vicegerent. ALUSI-FULL (الخليفة الأعظم): The report from the supreme vicegerent says God created Adam in His image, or in the image of the Merciful; the author uses it to explain Adam's comprehensiveness. ALUSI-FULL (author): Adam is God's vicegerent in the earth; prophets likewise succeed him in cultivating earth, governing people, perfecting souls, and carrying out God's command, not from divine need. ALUSI-FULL (أهل الله): The people of God identify Adam as God's vicegerent, father of later vicegerents, and comprehensive locus of divine beauty and majesty; both of God's hands are thereby described as right hands. ALUSI-FULL (قيل): Another explanation is that prophets and righteous people will descend from the vicegerent. SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Al-Baqara opens the Adam story at its earliest event: before Adam is created, God tells the angels He will appoint a vicegerent on earth. SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): The story honors Adam through his appointment as earth’s vicegerent, his being taught all the names beyond the angels’ knowledge, and the angels’ prostration to him. [8 notes: NASAFI-FULL; ALUSI-FULL(أهل الله,الخليفة الأعظم,السادة الصوفية,قيل); SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:30/q18/p5 Adam succeeds the earlier jinn — WAHIDI-WAJIZ (author): The khalīfa is Adam, successor to the angels who had inhabited the earth after the jinn; the story recalls the beginning of human creation. BAGHAWI-FULL (قيل): One view identifies the khalifa as Adam because he came after the jinn; another says he is called khalifa because others will succeed him. [2 notes: WAHIDI-WAJIZ; BAGHAWI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q18/p6 Al-Hasan says Adam’s children are called successors because each generation follows the preceding one; Ibn Atiyya says this can mean a succeeding or succeeded generation. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (الحسن): Al-Hasan says Adam’s children are called successors because each generation follows the preceding one; Ibn Atiyya says this can mean a succeeding or succeeded generation. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q18/p7 Being God’s servant is a greater honor than earthly caliphate: Adam’s caliph title did not deter Satan, but Satan has no power over those God calls His servants. — RAZI-FULL (author): Being God’s servant is a greater honor than earthly caliphate: Adam’s caliph title did not deter Satan, but Satan has no power over those God calls His servants. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p8 Abū Ḥayyān says God singled Adam out by announcing a khalifa, unlike the creation of the heavens, throne, or Paradise, thereby honoring and distinguishing him. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Abū Ḥayyān says God singled Adam out by announcing a khalifa, unlike the creation of the heavens, throne, or Paradise, thereby honoring and distinguishing him. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p9 In Ibn ʿAbbās's account, God gave Iblis earthly and lower-heaven sovereignty and Paradise's treasury; pride led him to think he was God's most honored angel, so the announced successor would replace him and his host. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (ابن عباس): In Ibn ʿAbbās's account, God gave Iblis earthly and lower-heaven sovereignty and Paradise's treasury; pride led him to think he was God's most honored angel, so the announced successor would replace him and his host. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q18/p10 Al-Ḥasan says the khalifa is Adam's progeny, each generation replacing another; the singular can represent a collective, as in other Qurʾanic passages. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (الحسن): Al-Ḥasan says the khalifa is Adam's progeny, each generation replacing another; the singular can represent a collective, as in other Qurʾanic passages. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q18/p11 The second purpose is to honor Adam by announcing his coming to the inhabitants of the divine realm and naming him khalīfa before his creation. — BURSEVI (author): The second purpose is to honor Adam by announcing his coming to the inhabitants of the divine realm and naming him khalīfa before his creation. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p12 One purpose of calling Adam caliph is to show that God graciously accept — BURSEVI (author): One purpose of calling Adam caliph is to show that God graciously accepted humanity for servanthood and caliphate, encouraging people not to despair but to continue serving. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p13 Hasan takes khalifa as successive generations. — TAB-FULL (author): Hasan takes khalifa as successive generations. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(الحسن البصري)]
- 2:30/q18/p14 Ibn Zayd says an empty earth would receive a created khalifa. — TAB-FULL (author): Ibn Zayd says an empty earth would receive a created khalifa. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن زيد)]
- 2:30/q18/p15 On the assertive reading, they may have inferred that a new vicegerent would corrupt the earth because the jinn before them had done so. — MAWARDI-FULL (author): On the assertive reading, they may have inferred that a new vicegerent would corrupt the earth because the jinn before them had done so. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p16 Khalīfa and imām overlap in use, but khalīfa succeeds another while imām leads a group and is to be obeyed. — JISHUMI (author): Khalīfa and imām overlap in use, but khalīfa succeeds another while imām leads a group and is to be obeyed. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q18/p17 The general reading is khalīfa with fāʾ. — JISHUMI (العامة): The general reading is khalīfa with fāʾ. [1 notes: JISHUMI(العامة)]
- 2:30/q18/p18 Thaʿlab and others say angels had witnessed the jinn’s corruption and bloodshed, so they asked whether the new successor would follow the jinn’s way. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (أحمد بن يحيى ثعلب): Thaʿlab and others say angels had witnessed the jinn’s corruption and bloodshed, so they asked whether the new successor would follow the jinn’s way. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(أحمد بن يحيى ثعلب)]
- 2:30/q18/p19 Ibn Abbas says jinn preceded Adam, corrupted the earth and shed blood; God sent angels who killed them and drove survivors to sea islands and mountain tops, then made Adam and his descendants successors. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says jinn preceded Adam, corrupted the earth and shed blood; God sent angels who killed them and drove survivors to sea islands and mountain tops, then made Adam and his descendants successors. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q18/p20 خليفة and إمام overlap in use but differ: a khalifa succeeds a predecessor, while an imam leads and is to be followed and obeyed. — TABRISI (author): خليفة and إمام overlap in use but differ: a khalifa succeeds a predecessor, while an imam leads and is to be followed and obeyed. [1 notes: TABRISI]
- 2:30/q18/p21 A khalifa succeeds another and takes that one's place; Razi illustrates this with verses about successors on earth. — RAZI-FULL (author): A khalifa succeeds another and takes that one's place; Razi illustrates this with verses about successors on earth. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p22 One view takes the successor to be Adam, with his descendants, rather than Adam himself, intended by the prediction of corruption. — RAZI-FULL (author): One view takes the successor to be Adam, with his descendants, rather than Adam himself, intended by the prediction of corruption. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p23 If they are asking to remain in heaven rather than have a human vicegerent on earth, God says their welfare lies in being in heaven, not on earth. — RAZI-FULL (author): If they are asking to remain in heaven rather than have a human vicegerent on earth, God says their welfare lies in being in heaven, not on earth. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p24 Some exegetes, whom al-Qurtubi attributes to Ibn Masud, Ibn Abbas, and all interpreters, take the khalifa to mean Adam alone. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (ابن مسعود وابن عباس وجميع أهل التأويل): Some exegetes, whom al-Qurtubi attributes to Ibn Masud, Ibn Abbas, and all interpreters, take the khalifa to mean Adam alone. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن مسعود وابن عباس وجميع أهل التأويل)]
- 2:30/q18/p25 Ibn Jarir says a ruler is called khalifa because he succeeds his predecessor and takes charge in his place. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (ابن جرير): Ibn Jarir says a ruler is called khalifa because he succeeds his predecessor and takes charge in his place. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن جرير)]
- 2:30/q18/p26 One explanation supplies a description of the appointed successor's future conduct; otherwise the angels would have had no basis to predict corruption, and the remaining text confirms the omission. — BURHAN (قيل): One explanation supplies a description of the appointed successor's future conduct; otherwise the angels would have had no basis to predict corruption, and the remaining text confirms the omission. [1 notes: BURHAN(قيل)]
- 2:30/q18/p27 An unattributed gloss says God's undisclosed knowledge concerned Iblis and the purpose for which the successor was created. — DURR-FULL (author): An unattributed gloss says God's undisclosed knowledge concerned Iblis and the purpose for which the successor was created. [1 notes: DURR-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p28 God preserves the world through a single pole-like khalīfa in each age: Adam began the succession and Jesus will end it. — BURSEVI (author): God preserves the world through a single pole-like khalīfa in each age: Adam began the succession and Jesus will end it. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p29 The seeker should show humility before God, His caliphs, shaykhs, and scholars, avoiding ego and displays of knowledge; one pursuing annihilation should not admire the self. — BURSEVI (author): The seeker should show humility before God, His caliphs, shaykhs, and scholars, avoiding ego and displays of knowledge; one pursuing annihilation should not admire the self. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q18/p30 The author explains the need for a vicegerent by the recipient's dense bodily nature and God's transcendence; under the divine order, an intermediary with both transcendent and attached aspects receives and transmits emanation. — ALUSI-FULL (author): The author explains the need for a vicegerent by the recipient's dense bodily nature and God's transcendence; under the divine order, an intermediary with both transcendent and attached aspects receives and transmits emanation. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p31 As a locus of divine majesty and beauty, the vicegerent can manifest both; corruption and bloodshed are effects of majesty, while the angels left the unremarkable effects of beauty unmentioned. — ALUSI-FULL (author): As a locus of divine majesty and beauty, the vicegerent can manifest both; corruption and bloodshed are effects of majesty, while the angels left the unremarkable effects of beauty unmentioned. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p32 Successors will carry out the same assigned duty, as expressed in the statement that God made people successors on earth. — ELMALILI (author): Successors will carry out the same assigned duty, as expressed in the statement that God made people successors on earth. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q18/p33 The account's meaning is the distilled substance of extensive interpretations transmitted from the Companions and Successors. — ELMALILI (ashâb-ı kirâm ve tâbiîn): The account's meaning is the distilled substance of extensive interpretations transmitted from the Companions and Successors. [1 notes: ELMALILI(ashâb-ı kirâm ve tâbiîn)]
- 2:30/q18/p34 The angels infer that an earthly successor must be numerous and socially organized, while embodied life and competition make corruption and bloodshed unavoidable. — TABATABAI (author): The angels infer that an earthly successor must be numerous and socially organized, while embodied life and competition make corruption and bloodshed unavoidable. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q18/p35 Instead, God reveals a divine secret that angels cannot bear but the earthly successor can; this capacity makes up for the successor's corruption and bloodshed. — TABATABAI (author): Instead, God reveals a divine secret that angels cannot bear but the earthly successor can; this capacity makes up for the successor's corruption and bloodshed. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q18/p36 Asad connects this reading to verses that call human beings khalaʾif al-arḍ, successors on earth. — ASAD-NOTES (author): Asad connects this reading to verses that call human beings khalaʾif al-arḍ, successors on earth. [1 notes: ASAD-NOTES]
- 2:30/q18/p37 Appointment as God's vicegerent is itself an honor, since the one entrusted with vicegerency must hold an elevated status. — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Appointment as God's vicegerent is itself an honor, since the one entrusted with vicegerency must hold an elevated status. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:30/q18/p38 Here and in 6:165, khalīfah suggests universal human inheritance and responsibility: all people are, in their inner reality, God’s vicegerents. — STUDYQURAN (author): Here and in 6:165, khalīfah suggests universal human inheritance and responsibility: all people are, in their inner reality, God’s vicegerents. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN]
- 2:30/q18/p39 Tabari says this may mean generations or a deputy judging by God's judgment. — TAB-FULL (author): Tabari says this may mean generations or a deputy judging by God's judgment. [1 notes: TAB-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p40 Tabari distinguishes God's deputy from Hasan's successive generations. — TAB-FULL (author): Tabari distinguishes God's deputy from Hasan's successive generations. [1 notes: TAB-FULL]
- 2:30/q18/p41 One view says Adam is God’s deputy for establishing His law, signs of His oneness, and judgment among His creation. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (ابن مسعود ومجاهد): One view says Adam is God’s deputy for establishing His law, signs of His oneness, and judgment among His creation. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن مسعود ومجاهد)]
- 2:30/q18/p42 Another view, attributed to Ibn Masud, Ibn Abbas, and al-Suddi, makes Adam God's deputy judging accountable creatures; 38:26 supports it. — RAZI-FULL (ابن مسعود وابن عباس والسدي): Another view, attributed to Ibn Masud, Ibn Abbas, and al-Suddi, makes Adam God's deputy judging accountable creatures; 38:26 supports it. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن مسعود وابن عباس والسدي)]
- 2:30/q18/p43 Deputyship may arise from the principal's absence, need, or incapacity, or simply from the principal honoring a deputy despite none of those causes. — ELMALILI (Râgıb): Deputyship may arise from the principal's absence, need, or incapacity, or simply from the principal honoring a deputy despite none of those causes. [1 notes: ELMALILI(Râgıb)]
- 2:30/q18/p44 A deputy's worth and honor are proportionate to the principal's honor and the degree of representation. — ELMALILI (author): A deputy's worth and honor are proportionate to the principal's honor and the degree of representation. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q18/p45 The promise to establish a community as stewards is conditioned on faith and righteous conduct, supporting the interpretation of stewardship as service under God. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): The promise to establish a community as stewards is conditioned on faith and righteous conduct, supporting the interpretation of stewardship as service under God. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q18/p46 Adam and his descendants exercise stewardship by living on God's earth in accordance with God's will and instructions. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Adam and his descendants exercise stewardship by living on God's earth in accordance with God's will and instructions. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q18/p47 Tabari rejects saying angels blamed the khalifa himself. — TAB-FULL (author): Tabari rejects saying angels blamed the khalifa himself. [0 notes: TAB-FULL-]

## 2:30/q19 What work and authority does the khalīfa have?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · interpretation]
- 2:30/q19/p1 A khalifa is one entrusted to carry out an order on behalf of the one who commands. — THALABI (author): A khalifa is one entrusted to carry out an order on behalf of the one who commands. [1 notes: THALABI]
- 2:30/q19/p2 Al-Hasan al-Basri takes خليفة as Adam's descendants, who succeed one another and Adam in upholding justice and cultivating earth. — TABRISI (الحسن البصري): Al-Hasan al-Basri takes خليفة as Adam's descendants, who succeed one another and Adam in upholding justice and cultivating earth. [1 notes: TABRISI(الحسن البصري)]
- 2:30/q19/p3 A khalifa as God's deputy judges people; because judges are needed for disputes, announcing a successor implied future conflict. — RAZI-FULL (author): A khalifa as God's deputy judges people; because judges are needed for disputes, announcing a successor implied future conflict. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q19/p4 The human vicegerent takes on God's traits, executes His command, governs creation, orders its affairs, and calls people to obedience. — QASHANI (author): The human vicegerent takes on God's traits, executes His command, governs creation, orders its affairs, and calls people to obedience. [1 notes: QASHANI]
- 2:30/q19/p5 Another view treats khalifa as the title of anyone entrusted with governing earth's people and overseeing their interests, as Caesar, Kisrā, and Tubbaʿ are royal titles. — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (author): Another view treats khalifa as the title of anyone entrusted with governing earth's people and overseeing their interests, as Caesar, Kisrā, and Tubbaʿ are royal titles. [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL]
- 2:30/q19/p6 Al-Qurtubi suggests the angels may have understood khalifa as one who judges among people and restrains wrongs and violations. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (القرطبي): Al-Qurtubi suggests the angels may have understood khalifa as one who judges among people and restrains wrongs and violations. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(القرطبي)]
- 2:30/q19/p7 Khilāfa, like agency, is deputation in place of principal authority: taking another's place to some extent and representing that principal. — ELMALILI (author): Khilāfa, like agency, is deputation in place of principal authority: taking another's place to some extent and representing that principal. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q19/p8 The vicegerent has no authority in his own right: he is a deputy charged to apply God's will, commands, and laws. — ELMALILI (author): The vicegerent has no authority in his own right: he is a deputy charged to apply God's will, commands, and laws. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q19/p9 A vicegerent must represent God in all existential affairs, effects, laws, and governance; the flawed earthly being appears unfit to represent the perfectly pure God. — TABATABAI (author): A vicegerent must represent God in all existential affairs, effects, laws, and governance; the flawed earthly being appears unfit to represent the perfectly pure God. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q19/p10 Ibn Jarir interprets the khalifa as God's deputy in judging among creation: Adam and those who obey God and rule justly; unjust corruption and bloodshed come from others. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (ابن جرير): Ibn Jarir interprets the khalifa as God's deputy in judging among creation: Adam and those who obey God and rule justly; unjust corruption and bloodshed come from others. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(ابن جرير)]
- 2:30/q19/p11 Baghawi prefers that Adam is God's deputy on earth, charged with establishing His rulings and carrying out His commands. — BAGHAWI-FULL (author): Baghawi prefers that Adam is God's deputy on earth, charged with establishing His rulings and carrying out His commands. [1 notes: BAGHAWI-FULL+]
- 2:30/q19/p12 Ibn Masud says Adam is God’s deputy for judging servants by truth and His commands, a role continued by his descendants. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (ابن مسعود): Ibn Masud says Adam is God’s deputy for judging servants by truth and His commands, a role continued by his descendants. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL(ابن مسعود)]
- 2:30/q19/p13 General stewardship is not exclusive to Adam; other verses explicitly extend this capacity and authority to all people. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): General stewardship is not exclusive to Adam; other verses explicitly extend this capacity and authority to all people. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q19/p14 Human stewardship is limited to human nature and capacities; no creature can represent God, take God's place, or exercise God's authority. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Human stewardship is limited to human nature and capacities; no creature can represent God, take God's place, or exercise God's authority. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]

## 2:30/q20 What happened on earth before Adam?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · reports]
- 2:30/q20/p1 Jinn inhabited earth, corrupted it, and were defeated by angels before Adam — MUQATIL (author): Jinn first inhabited earth, angels heaven; envious, fighting jinn were defeated by a fiery host under Iblis. MUQATIL (author): Corruption means sins and bloodshed wrongfully, as earlier jinn did. TAB-FULL (author): Ibn Abbas reports jinn first corrupted earth and Adam succeeded them. ZAJJAJ (قيل): A people called jinn lived on earth, corrupted it and shed blood; God sent angels to drive them away, and the angels then inhabited earth. THALABI (المفسرون): Exegetes recount that jinn first worshipped on earth, then envy and aggression led them to fight and corrupt it. THALABI (المفسرون): An angelic force called jinn, led by Iblis, expelled the earlier jinn to mountains and sea islands and took over earth. THALABI (أكثر المفسرين): Most exegetes say the angels inferred human corruption by analogy with the earlier jinn, reasoning from the witnessed to the unseen. MAWARDI-FULL (author): Alternatively, the question form expresses an assertion; the angels inferred corruption from the jinn who had inhabited earth before. TABRISI (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas reports that jinn lived on earth, corrupted it and shed blood, were destroyed, and were replaced by Adam and his descendants. QURTUBI-FULL (أحمد بن يحيى ثعلب): Thaʿlab says the angels had seen jinn corrupt and shed blood on earth before Adam; God sent Satan with an angelic host to kill them and drive them to seas and mountains, so they asked whether the human successor would be like the jinn. NASAFI-FULL (author): Before Adam, jinn inhabited earth and corrupted it; angels expelled them to islands, seas, and mountaintops, then settled there. ABUHAYYAN-FULL (ابن عباس): A report from Ibn ʿAbbās says God first placed angels in heaven and jinn on earth; after the jinn corrupted and fought, Iblis led angels who expelled them and took earthly rule. IBNKATHIR-FULL (الضحاك عن ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas, through al-Dahhak, says jinn first inhabited earth and shed blood; God sent Iblis to defeat and drive them to remote places, then created Adam to succeed them. IBNKATHIR-FULL (الضحاك عن ابن عباس): Jinn had corrupted the earth before Adam’s descendants, and the angels compared the new beings with them. DURR-FULL (ابن عباس): In Ibn Abbas's account, jinn first inhabited earth and fought, shed blood, and were defeated by Iblis and an angel army, who drove them to islands and mountain edges. BURSEVI (author): After a long period, the jinn grew envious and oppressive, corrupted the earth, and killed; God sent the lowest-heaven angels under Iblis to defeat and expel them. STUDYQURAN (author): Prior jinn violence offers one possible explanation for the angels’ question about placing a corrupt, blood-shedding being on earth. STUDYQURAN (Ibn ʿAbbās): In the same report, jinn first inhabited earth and shed blood; God sent Iblīs with the al-ḥinn against them, driving them to islands and mountain edges, and Iblīs grew proud of the victory. [18 notes: MUQATIL; TAB-FULL(ابن عباس); ZAJJAJ(قيل); THALABI(أكثر المفسرين,المفسرون); MAWARDI-FULL; TABRISI(ابن عباس); QURTUBI-FULL(أحمد بن يحيى ثعلب); NASAFI-FULL; ABUHAYYAN-FULL(ابن عباس); IBNKATHIR-FULL(الضحاك عن ابن عباس); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس); BURSEVI; STUDYQURAN(Ibn ʿAbbās)]
- 2:30/q20/p2 Jinn lived on earth two thousand years before Adam and were expelled — IBNABIHATIM (عبد الله بن عمرو): Abdullah ibn Amr says jinn inhabited earth two thousand years before Adam, spread corruption and bloodshed, and were driven to sea islands by an angelic force; this explains the angels' question. IBNKATHIR-FULL (عبد الله بن عمرو): A report says jinn lived on earth two thousand years before Adam, shed blood and caused corruption; angels drove them to sea islands, after which God announced Adam’s succession. DURR-FULL (ابن عباس): A report authenticated by al-Hakim says jinn inhabited earth for two thousand years before Adam, corrupted it and shed blood; angels drove them away, prompting their question about a successor. [3 notes: IBNABIHATIM(عبد الله بن عمرو); IBNKATHIR-FULL(عبد الله بن عمرو); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q20/p3 Angels were created Wednesday, jinn Thursday, and Adam Friday — TAB-FULL (author): Rabi says angels fought rebellious jinn after their creation on Thursday. IBNABIHATIM (أبو العالية): Abu al-Aliya says angels were created Wednesday, jinn Thursday, and Adam Friday; rebellious jinn fought angels, so the angels compared humans' coming corruption and bloodshed to the jinn's. [2 notes: TAB-FULL(الربيع بن أنس); IBNABIHATIM(أبو العالية)]
- 2:30/q20/p4 Al-Dahhak, from Ibn Abbas and also followed by al-Rabi b. Anas, says angels knew of the jinn who had lived on earth before Adam and asked whether later creatures would act like them; their question was — TAB-FULL (الضحاك عن ابن عباس والربيع بن أنس): Al-Dahhak, from Ibn Abbas and also followed by al-Rabi b. Anas, says angels knew of the jinn who had lived on earth before Adam and asked whether later creatures would act like them; their question was inquiry, not certainty. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(الضحاك عن ابن عباس والربيع بن أنس)]
- 2:30/q20/p5 It is related that creatures lived on earth before Adam, caused corruption, and were destroyed by Iblīs and a company of angels sent by God. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (author): It is related that creatures lived on earth before Adam, caused corruption, and were destroyed by Iblīs and a company of angels sent by God. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD]
- 2:30/q20/p6 The alternative view is that the angels inferred the corruption from the condition of those who had lived before Adam. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (ابن عباس وأبي العالية ومقاتل): The alternative view is that the angels inferred the corruption from the condition of those who had lived before Adam. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن عباس وأبي العالية ومقاتل)]
- 2:30/q20/p7 Ibn Abbas and al-Kalbi are cited for conjecture by analogy with the jinn who lived on earth before Adam. — RAZI-FULL (ابن عباس والكلبي): Ibn Abbas and al-Kalbi are cited for conjecture by analogy with the jinn who lived on earth before Adam. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL(ابن عباس والكلبي)]
- 2:30/q20/p8 A fourth view from Aḥmad ibn Yaḥyā takes the question plainly: will this successor be like the earlier jinn who inhabited the earth? — ABUHAYYAN-FULL (أحمد بن يحيى): A fourth view from Aḥmad ibn Yaḥyā takes the question plainly: will this successor be like the earlier jinn who inhabited the earth? [1 notes: ABUHAYYAN-FULL(أحمد بن يحيى)]
- 2:30/q20/p9 Ibn Umar is reported as giving the same account as Ibn Abbas about the earlier jinn and the angels' question. — DURR-FULL (ابن عمر): Ibn Umar is reported as giving the same account as Ibn Abbas about the earlier jinn and the angels' question. [1 notes: DURR-FULL(ابن عمر)]
- 2:30/q20/p10 The account says jinn, children of al-Jānn, were created from smokeless flame between heaven and earth and inhabited earth long before Adam. — BURSEVI (author): The account says jinn, children of al-Jānn, were created from smokeless flame between heaven and earth and inhabited earth long before Adam. [1 notes: BURSEVI]
- 2:30/q20/p11 Many commentators accept that jinn inhabited earth before human beings. — STUDYQURAN (many commentators): Many commentators accept that jinn inhabited earth before human beings. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN(many commentators)]
- 2:30/q20/p12 Jinn on earth resented being replaced because their worship duties were lighter. — MUQATIL (author): Jinn on earth resented being replaced because their worship duties were lighter. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q20/p13 خليفة can mean a replacement for those who came before, here replacing the jinn. — DAMGHANI (author): خليفة can mean a replacement for those who came before, here replacing the jinn. [1 notes: DAMGHANI]
- 2:30/q20/p14 The enmity belongs to unbelieving jinn, not believers; unbelievers oppose human believers whether jinn or human. Another view makes it inherited without cause, and the author asks what caused Iblīs to refuse Adam's prostration. — JISHUMI (author): The enmity belongs to unbelieving jinn, not believers; unbelievers oppose human believers whether jinn or human. Another view makes it inherited without cause, and the author asks what caused Iblīs to refuse Adam's prostration. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q20/p15 A further view says the addressees were Iblīs and angels who had fought rebellious jinn inhabiting the earth, driving them to mountain passes and islands. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): A further view says the addressees were Iblīs and angels who had fought rebellious jinn inhabiting the earth, driving them to mountain passes and islands. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q20/p16 On this descendants interpretation, humans succeed earlier earth-dwellers, whether the jinn, Iblīs and his companions, or they succeed one another. — ALUSI-FULL (قيل): On this descendants interpretation, humans succeed earlier earth-dwellers, whether the jinn, Iblīs and his companions, or they succeed one another. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q20/p17 Some commentators analogized to predatory beasts that lived on earth before Adam, as in Genesis; Ibn ʿĀshūr likewise treats this as unnecessary. — IBNASHUR-FULL (بعض المفسرين): Some commentators analogized to predatory beasts that lived on earth before Adam, as in Genesis; Ibn ʿĀshūr likewise treats this as unnecessary. [0 notes: IBNASHUR-FULL(بعض المفسرين)-]

## 2:30/q21 What is reported about Adam's formation and first acts?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · reports]
- 2:30/q21/p1 Adam's red and white clay from salt and fresh soil explains varied colors and faith among descendants. — MUQATIL (author): Adam's red and white clay from salt and fresh soil explains varied colors and faith among descendants. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q21/p2 Adam's shaped clay lay forty years; Iblis entered and left the hollow body, boasting fire would overcome clay. — MUQATIL (author): Adam's shaped clay lay forty years; Iblis entered and left the hollow body, boasting fire would overcome clay. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q21/p3 The spirit entered Adam's head, reached his navel and toes, and Adam sneezed and first praised God. — MUQATIL (author): The spirit entered Adam's head, reached his navel and toes, and Adam sneezed and first praised God. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q21/p4 Adam was made from clay of divine might and Muhammad's light. — TUSTARI (author): Adam was made from clay of divine might and Muhammad's light. [1 notes: TUSTARI]
- 2:30/q21/p5 Ibn Sabit identifies corrupt blood-shedders as Adam's children. — TAB-FULL (author): Ibn Sabit identifies corrupt blood-shedders as Adam's children. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن سابط)]
- 2:30/q21/p6 Ibn Abbas says Adam was formed from sticky clay, lay forty nights, and Iblis entered him. — TAB-FULL (author): Ibn Abbas says Adam was formed from sticky clay, lay forty nights, and Iblis entered him. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q21/p7 In Ibn Abbas’s report, Adam’s haste is linked to human impatience; he praises God at a sneeze, Iblis refuses prostration from pride and is expelled, then Adam names things, while the angels disclaim unseen knowledge and — TAB-FULL (ابن عباس): In Ibn Abbas’s report, Adam’s haste is linked to human impatience; he praises God at a sneeze, Iblis refuses prostration from pride and is expelled, then Adam names things, while the angels disclaim unseen knowledge and God exposes Iblis’s concealed pride. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q21/p8 Al-Suddi’s report from Ibn Abbas, Ibn Masud and Companions says Iblis ruled a heaven, grew proud, and angels were told the khalīfa’s descendants would corrupt, envy and kill; their question followed. The account then tells how — TAB-FULL (السدي عن أبي مالك وأبي صالح وابن عباس وابن مسعود وأصحاب النبي): Al-Suddi’s report from Ibn Abbas, Ibn Masud and Companions says Iblis ruled a heaven, grew proud, and angels were told the khalīfa’s descendants would corrupt, envy and kill; their question followed. The account then tells how angels collected multicolored clay despite earth’s pleas, explaining human diversity, and Adam was formed from it. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(السدي عن أبي مالك وأبي صالح وابن عباس وابن مسعود وأصحاب النبي)]
- 2:30/q21/p9 Ibn Zayd relates that angels, afraid of hell, wondered whether they might disobey; God announced a human population of corruption, Adam’s knowledge proved his superiority, and Iblis refused to yield, citing fire versus clay. — TAB-FULL (ابن زيد): Ibn Zayd relates that angels, afraid of hell, wondered whether they might disobey; God announced a human population of corruption, Adam’s knowledge proved his superiority, and Iblis refused to yield, citing fire versus clay. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن زيد)]
- 2:30/q21/p10 Ibn Ishaq says Adam was formed from earth and God’s hands for honor, lay forty years before ensoulment and became pottery-like without fire; he sneezed at ensoulment, angels prostrated, but proud, envious Iblis refused. — TAB-FULL (ابن إسحاق): Ibn Ishaq says Adam was formed from earth and God’s hands for honor, lay forty years before ensoulment and became pottery-like without fire; he sneezed at ensoulment, angels prostrated, but proud, envious Iblis refused. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن إسحاق)]
- 2:30/q21/p11 The Adam narrative says clay was gathered from every region and left to ferment for forty mornings; the angels wondered what its destiny would be, then marveled at his fashioned form. — QUSHAYRI (author): The Adam narrative says clay was gathered from every region and left to ferment for forty mornings; the angels wondered what its destiny would be, then marveled at his fashioned form. [1 notes: QUSHAYRI]
- 2:30/q21/p12 The Prophet said Adam was sixty cubits tall; the report is in the Ṣaḥīḥs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم): The Prophet said Adam was sixty cubits tall; the report is in the Ṣaḥīḥs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم)]
- 2:30/q21/p13 Mention of earth implies a human made from clay; the angels wondered whether he would misuse delegated authority as if it were his own and cause corruption. — ELMALILI (author): Mention of earth implies a human made from clay; the angels wondered whether he would misuse delegated authority as if it were his own and cause corruption. [1 notes: ELMALILI]
- 2:30/q21/p14 The report says Adam’s clay body remained lifeless for forty years; Iblīs entered and taunted it, threatening to destroy or defy Adam if either gained power over the other. — STUDYQURAN (Ibn ʿAbbās): The report says Adam’s clay body remained lifeless for forty years; Iblīs entered and taunted it, threatening to destroy or defy Adam if either gained power over the other. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN(Ibn ʿAbbās)]
- 2:30/q21/p15 In the account, God commands only the angels accompanying Iblīs to prostrate; Iblīs refuses, claiming superiority by age and greatness and contrasting his fire with Adam’s clay. — STUDYQURAN (Ibn ʿAbbās): In the account, God commands only the angels accompanying Iblīs to prostrate; Iblīs refuses, claiming superiority by age and greatness and contrasting his fire with Adam’s clay. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN(Ibn ʿAbbās)]
- 2:30/q21/p16 The Quran’s other indication of this order of creation is 15:26–27: human beings were made from molded clay and jinn earlier from scorching fire. — STUDYQURAN (author): The Quran’s other indication of this order of creation is 15:26–27: human beings were made from molded clay and jinn earlier from scorching fire. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN]
- 2:30/q21/p17 Adam's spirit resisted entering the dark body and was told it would enter and leave unwillingly. — MUQATIL (author): Adam's spirit resisted entering the dark body and was told it would enter and leave unwillingly. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q21/p18 Adam's early sitting illustrates human haste in 17:11. — MUQATIL (author): Adam's early sitting illustrates human haste in 17:11. [1 notes: MUQATIL]
- 2:30/q21/p19 When asked whether Adam was created for heaven or earth, Hasan answers that he was created for earth, citing this verse. — IBNABIHATIM (الحسن): When asked whether Adam was created for heaven or earth, Hasan answers that he was created for earth, citing this verse. [1 notes: IBNABIHATIM(الحسن)]
- 2:30/q21/p20 Al-Mufaḍḍal says the verse recalls God’s favor to Adam after the preceding reminder of what He created for people on earth. — MAWARDI-FULL (المفضل): Al-Mufaḍḍal says the verse recalls God’s favor to Adam after the preceding reminder of what He created for people on earth. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL(المفضل)]
- 2:30/q21/p21 Al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī takes it as generations of Adam’s children succeeding one another in upholding right and cultivating earth. — MAWARDI-FULL (الحسن البصري): Al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī takes it as generations of Adam’s children succeeding one another in upholding right and cultivating earth. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL(الحسن البصري)]
- 2:30/q21/p22 Ibn ʿAbbās and Ibn Masʿūd say God refers to Iblīs’s concealed pride and disobedience when commanded to prostrate to Adam. — MAWARDI-FULL (ابن عباس وابن مسعود): Ibn ʿAbbās and Ibn Masʿūd say God refers to Iblīs’s concealed pride and disobedience when commanded to prostrate to Adam. [1 notes: MAWARDI-FULL(ابن عباس وابن مسعود)]
- 2:30/q21/p23 One account connects the verse to preceding favors: human creation and life, earthly things, heaven, then Adam and blessings to his descendants. — JISHUMI (author): One account connects the verse to preceding favors: human creation and life, earthly things, heaven, then Adam and blessings to his descendants. [1 notes: JISHUMI]
- 2:30/q21/p24 Iblīs took his success as proof of superiority and, in relation to Adam, incurred unbelief and disobedience. — IBNATIYYA-FULL (author): Iblīs took his success as proof of superiority and, in relation to Adam, incurred unbelief and disobedience. [1 notes: IBNATIYYA-FULL]
- 2:30/q21/p25 The other view says Adam succeeded earlier inhabitants of the earth. — IBNJAWZI-ZAD (ابن عباس والحسن): The other view says Adam succeeded earlier inhabitants of the earth. [1 notes: IBNJAWZI-ZAD(ابن عباس والحسن)]
- 2:30/q21/p26 The verse recounts Adam's creation and God's honoring of him, a general favor to all humanity and the third general blessing in this passage. — RAZI-FULL (author): The verse recounts Adam's creation and God's honoring of him, a general favor to all humanity and the third general blessing in this passage. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q21/p27 Adam was sent to his own forty children, born as twenty pairs of a boy and a girl, whose descendants multiplied. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Adam was sent to his own forty children, born as twenty pairs of a boy and a girl, whose descendants multiplied. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q21/p28 The account of Adam’s offspring follows the verse about humanity’s single origin and its spouse. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The account of Adam’s offspring follows the verse about humanity’s single origin and its spouse. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q21/p29 Biqai reads Adam’s public creation story as a manifest sign concealing the more elevated, privately understood account of Muhammad’s creat — BIQAI-FULL (author): Biqai reads Adam’s public creation story as a manifest sign concealing the more elevated, privately understood account of Muhammad’s creation. [1 notes: BIQAI-FULL]
- 2:30/q21/p30 The account places the Kursī inside the Throne, creates nested heavens and their inhabitants, and develops generated forms, spirits, bodies, and fitting sustenance. — ALUSI-FULL (أهل الله): The account places the Kursī inside the Throne, creates nested heavens and their inhabitants, and develops generated forms, spirits, bodies, and fitting sustenance. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(أهل الله)]
- 2:30/q21/p31 Ibn al-Fāriḍ's verse voices the Muhammadan Reality: although outwardly a son of Adam, Muhammad bears an inner meaning that witnesses to his fatherhood. — ALUSI-FULL (ابن الفارض): Ibn al-Fāriḍ's verse voices the Muhammadan Reality: although outwardly a son of Adam, Muhammad bears an inner meaning that witnesses to his fatherhood. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(ابن الفارض)]
- 2:30/q21/p32 This angel knew Adam's creation and rank because it inscribed all that would be on the Tablet, which experientially knew what the Pen wrote. — ALUSI-FULL (السادة الصوفية): This angel knew Adam's creation and rank because it inscribed all that would be on the Tablet, which experientially knew what the Pen wrote. [1 notes: ALUSI-FULL(السادة الصوفية)]
- 2:30/q21/p33 Divine vicegerency is shared by Adam's descendants; teaching the names deposits knowledge in humanity so its effects can unfold gradually and become actual through guidance. — TABATABAI (author): Divine vicegerency is shared by Adam's descendants; teaching the names deposits knowledge in humanity so its effects can unfold gradually and become actual through guidance. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q21/p34 Qur'anic references to peoples succeeding earlier peoples support the view that earthly vicegerency is general to humanity, not exclusive to Adam. — TABATABAI (author): Qur'anic references to peoples succeeding earlier peoples support the view that earthly vicegerency is general to humanity, not exclusive to Adam. [1 notes: TABATABAI]
- 2:30/q21/p35 One possible referent is earlier conscious beings who lived on earth, whom Adam and humanity succeeded. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): One possible referent is earlier conscious beings who lived on earth, whom Adam and humanity succeeded. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q21/p36 Successful vicegerency requires authority to administer its domain and proven capacity; God gave humanity the earth, Adam's test showed his fitness, and the chooser knows the unseen. — SAMARRAI-ASRAR (author): Successful vicegerency requires authority to administer its domain and proven capacity; God gave humanity the earth, Adam's test showed his fitness, and the chooser knows the unseen. [1 notes: SAMARRAI-ASRAR]
- 2:30/q21/p37 Eve enters without an account of her origin; although primordial Adam represents all humanity, male and female, the Quran does not explain how this Adam becomes the first couple. — STUDYQURAN (author): Eve enters without an account of her origin; although primordial Adam represents all humanity, male and female, the Quran does not explain how this Adam becomes the first couple. [1 notes: STUDYQURAN]

## 2:30/q22 What is reported about Iblis's earlier status and hidden pride?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · reports]
- 2:30/q22/p1 Iblis envied or scorned Adam and refused God's command — MUQATIL (author): Iblis envied Adam's form; angels promised obedience, while Iblis privately resolved to disobey and mislead. TAB-FULL (مجاهد): Mujahid also says God knew Iblis concealed pride and would refuse to prostrate to Adam. WAHIDI-BASIT (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas and most commentators say angels compared Adam's descendants with corrupting jinn; God knew Iblis's pride and delusion, and angels later admitted ignorance when unable to name things. TABRISI (ابن عباس وابن مسعود): One view is that God knows Satan's concealed pride, vanity, and disobedience when ordered to prostrate to Adam. DURR-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says Iblis belonged to a group called jinn among the angels, was made from scorching fire, and was named al-Harith; other angels were from light and jinn from flame. MUQATIL (author): Iblis's boast and plan for Adam's descendants are linked to 34:20 and 17:62, excepting a few believers. [6 notes: MUQATIL; TAB-FULL(مجاهد); WAHIDI-BASIT(ابن عباس); TABRISI(ابن عباس وابن مسعود); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q22/p2 Iblis held authority over earth or heaven and became proud — WAHIDI-BASIT (author): The account says God had given Iblis dominion over earth, the lower heaven, and the gardens’ treasuries; he worshipped in these places, grew proud, and God disclosed his arrogance before announcing a successor on earth. BAGHAWI-FULL (author): God gave Iblis rule over earth, the lowest heaven, and the Garden treasury; his worship across these places led him to pride and the belief that he was God's most honored angel. BURSEVI (author): The narrative gives Iblis the name ʿAzāzīl and describes his rule over earth, the lowest heaven, and Paradise's treasury; he became proud of God's favors and imagined himself most honored. QURTUBI-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn ʿAbbas says Iblis grew arrogant after being honored as heaven's treasurer, assumed his rank made him superior, and scorned Adam's disobedience; the angels did not know Iblis's contrary inner state. [4 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT; BAGHAWI-FULL; BURSEVI; QURTUBI-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q22/p3 Iblis became proud after defeating the jinn — TAB-FULL (author): Ibn Abbas's account identifies Iblis as al-Harith of al-Hinn and describes the jinn's defeat and his hidden pride. TAB-FULL (author): The account continues: angels prostrate except proud Iblis, who is expelled; Adam names the creatures, the angels acknowledge they know only what God taught, and God reveals the pride Iblis concealed. DURR-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says Iblis became proud after defeating the jinn, while God knew the arrogance in his heart that the angels could not see. [3 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس); DURR-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q22/p4 He calls the angels’ question a lapse and conjecture about the unseen; they were shown their error, repented and disclaimed unseen knowledge, while God exposed Iblis’s hidden arrogance. — TAB-FULL (author): He calls the angels’ question a lapse and conjecture about the unseen; they were shown their error, repented and disclaimed unseen knowledge, while God exposed Iblis’s hidden arrogance. [1 notes: TAB-FULL]
- 2:30/q22/p5 Al-Ṭabarī says this report’s opening differs from the earlier Ibn Abbas account, while its ending agrees in the angels’ admission of ignorance and disclosure of Iblis’s pride. — TAB-FULL (author): Al-Ṭabarī says this report’s opening differs from the earlier Ibn Abbas account, while its ending agrees in the angels’ admission of ignorance and disclosure of Iblis’s pride. [1 notes: TAB-FULL]
- 2:30/q22/p6 Ibn Abbas says God knew that Iblis's heart concealed arrogance and self-delusion unknown to the angels. — TAB-FULL (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says God knew that Iblis's heart concealed arrogance and self-delusion unknown to the angels. [1 notes: TAB-FULL(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q22/p7 God's answer points to Iblis's hidden arrogance, unknown to the angels, and reproaches their universal claim about themselves when some among them would disobey. — TAB-FULL (author): God's answer points to Iblis's hidden arrogance, unknown to the angels, and reproaches their universal claim about themselves when some among them would disobey. [1 notes: TAB-FULL]
- 2:30/q22/p8 Mujahid says God knew Iblis would disobey and created him for that disobedience. — THALABI (مجاهد): Mujahid says God knew Iblis would disobey and created him for that disobedience. [1 notes: THALABI(مجاهد)]
- 2:30/q22/p9 Ibn Abbas says what God knew included Iblis’s concealed resolve to disobey and his pride. — WAHIDI-BASIT (ابن عباس): Ibn Abbas says what God knew included Iblis’s concealed resolve to disobey and his pride. [1 notes: WAHIDI-BASIT(ابن عباس)]
- 2:30/q22/p10 What God knew and the angels did not was Iblis’s concealed resolve to disobey. — WAHIDI-WAJIZ (author): What God knew and the angels did not was Iblis’s concealed resolve to disobey. [1 notes: WAHIDI-WAJIZ]
- 2:30/q22/p11 A force of angels called jinn, the keepers of the gardens, led by Iblis, drove the earthly jinn into mountains, valleys, and islands, then occupied earth. — BAGHAWI-FULL (author): A force of angels called jinn, the keepers of the gardens, led by Iblis, drove the earthly jinn into mountains, valleys, and islands, then occupied earth. [1 notes: BAGHAWI-FULL]
- 2:30/q22/p12 Another explanation is that an angel among them, Iblis, will disobey God. — BAGHAWI-FULL (قيل): Another explanation is that an angel among them, Iblis, will disobey God. [1 notes: BAGHAWI-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q22/p13 God may be revealing that Iblis is among the angels, with envy, pride, and hypocrisy in his heart. — RAZI-FULL (author): God may be revealing that Iblis is among the angels, with envy, pride, and hypocrisy in his heart. [1 notes: RAZI-FULL]
- 2:30/q22/p14 Repel Satan's harm by examining proofs, dispelling doubts, avoiding innovation and error, restraining desires, performing obedience, and avoiding major sins. — JISHUMI (author): Repel Satan's harm by examining proofs, dispelling doubts, avoiding innovation and error, restraining desires, performing obedience, and avoiding major sins. [1 notes: JISHUMI]

## 2:30/q23 Does the verse require appointing an imam?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · law]
- 2:30/q23/p1 Al-Asamm says appointment is not obligatory if the community performs pilgrimage and jihad, acts fairly, distributes public funds, and applies legal penalties. — QURTUBI-FULL (الأصم): Al-Asamm says appointment is not obligatory if the community performs pilgrimage and jihad, acts fairly, distributes public funds, and applies legal penalties. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الأصم)]
- 2:30/q23/p2 The Rafidites say reason makes appointment obligatory, with revelation only confirming this. — QURTUBI-FULL (الرافضة): The Rafidites say reason makes appointment obligatory, with revelation only confirming this. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الرافضة)]
- 2:30/q23/p3 If a small faction could deny necessary knowledge, established facts about Baghdad and far China could also be denied. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): If a small faction could deny necessary knowledge, established facts about Baghdad and far China could also be denied. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q23/p4 Al-Qurtubi and others infer that appointing an imam is obligatory to settle disputes, defend the oppressed, enforce penalties and deter indecencies, since necessary duties require an imam. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (القرطبي وغيره): Al-Qurtubi and others infer that appointing an imam is obligatory to settle disputes, defend the oppressed, enforce penalties and deter indecencies, since necessary duties require an imam. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(القرطبي وغيره)]
- 2:30/q23/p5 Reason neither obligates or forbids nor makes things good or bad; appointment is obligatory by revelation, not reason. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Reason neither obligates or forbids nor makes things good or bad; appointment is obligatory by revelation, not reason. [0 notes: QURTUBI-FULL-]

## 2:30/q24 How is an imam appointed?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · law]
- 2:30/q24/p1 The report of ʿUbada forbids disputing authority except upon clear unbelief for which there is proof from God; Muslim transmits it. — QURTUBI-FULL (النبي): The report of ʿUbada forbids disputing authority except upon clear unbelief for which there is proof from God; Muslim transmits it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(النبي)]
- 2:30/q24/p2 An absta — QURTUBI-FULL (author): An abstainer with an excuse is excused, while an unexcused person may be compelled so the Muslims do not divide. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q24/p3 A duty to obey one named imam would require the whole community to know him; neither reason nor transmission establishes a particular person. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): A duty to obey one named imam would require the whole community to know him; neither reason nor transmission establishes a particular person. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q24/p4 Abu Dawud and Ab — QURTUBI-FULL (أبو داود السجستاني وأبو حاتم الرازي): Abu Dawud and Abu Hatim argue the Ghadir report conflicts with the Prophet’s saying that four tribes are clients only of God and His Messenger. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو داود السجستاني وأبو حاتم الرازي)]
- 2:30/q24/p5 A third route is agreement of the people empowered to bind and loose in the imam's city; if no successor exists, their accepted choice binds Muslims elsewhere unless the imam openly manifests corruption. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): A third route is agreement of the people empowered to bind and loose in the imam's city; if no successor exists, their accepted choice binds Muslims elsewhere unless the imam openly manifests corruption. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q24/p6 The Prophet says sincerity to God, adherence to the community, and counsel to rulers keep rancor from a believer's heart, and the Muslims' call encompasses those behind them. — QURTUBI-FULL (رسول الله): The Prophet says sincerity to God, adherence to the community, and counsel to rulers keep rancor from a believer's heart, and the Muslims' call encompasses those behind them. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(رسول الله)]
- 2:30/q24/p7 The author rejects al-Jubbaʾi's extra requirements: both sides accept two witnesses, while the additional number has no supporting proof. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The author rejects al-Jubbaʾi's extra requirements: both sides accept two witnesses, while the additional number has no supporting proof. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 2:30/q24/p8 Asked about a ruler who overcomes the country, Sahl al-Tustari says to obey him, pay what he claims as his due, refrain from denouncing or fleeing him, and keep entrusted religious secrets. — QURTUBI-FULL (سهل بن عبد الله التستري): Asked about a ruler who overcomes the country, Sahl al-Tustari says to obey him, pay what he claims as his due, refrain from denouncing or fleeing him, and keep entrusted religious secrets. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(سهل بن عبد الله التستري)]
- 2:30/q24/p9 Some of the author's colleagues say witnesses are not required for the imamate contract because no decisive revealed proof establishes that requirement. — QURTUBI-FULL (بعض أصحابنا): Some of the author's colleagues say witnesses are not required for the imamate contract because no decisive revealed proof establishes that requirement. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(بعض أصحابنا)]
- 2:30/q24/p10 Others require witnesses to prevent secret competing claims and ensuing disorder, and consider two witnesses sufficient. — QURTUBI-FULL (بعض أصحابنا): Others require witnesses to prevent secret competing claims and ensuing disorder, and consider two witnesses sufficient. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(بعض أصحابنا)]
- 2:30/q24/p11 The majority say an imam who becomes manifestly sinful after appointment loses office, because sin disables the duties for which the imamate exists. — QURTUBI-FULL (الجمهور): The majority say an imam who becomes manifestly sinful after appointment loses office, because sin disables the duties for which the imamate exists. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الجمهور)]
- 2:30/q24/p12 Others say removal is permitted only for unbelief, abandoning prayer or its call, or abandoning a rule of the law; the supporting hadith begins in the next segment. — QURTUBI-FULL (آخرون): Others say removal is permitted only for unbelief, abandoning prayer or its call, or abandoning a rule of the law; the supporting hadith begins in the next segment. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(آخرون)]
- 2:30/q24/p13 A report says the imams are the community's intercessors, so people should consider whom they choose for intercession. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): A report says the imams are the community's intercessors, so people should consider whom they choose for intercession. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q24/p14 Qatada says God knew that Adam’s descendants would include prophets, messengers, righteous people and inhabitants of Paradise. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (قتادة): Qatada says God knew that Adam’s descendants would include prophets, messengers, righteous people and inhabitants of Paradise. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(قتادة)]
- 2:30/q24/p15 Another view requires testimony for the imamate contract, with two witnesses sufficient. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (قيل): Another view requires testimony for the imamate contract, with two witnesses sufficient. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(قيل)]
- 2:30/q24/p16 Another Sunni group holds that the imamate may be established by an indication or allusion. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (آخرون من أهل السنة): Another Sunni group holds that the imamate may be established by an indication or allusion. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(آخرون من أهل السنة)]

## 2:30/q25 What qualifications must an imam have?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · law]
- 2:30/q25/p1 The Companions agreed on Abu Bakr after Saqifa; the Ansar proposed one leader from each group, but were persuaded that Arabs accepted Quraysh. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The Companions agreed on Abu Bakr after Saqifa; the Ansar proposed one leader from each group, but were persuaded that Arabs accepted Quraysh. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p2 Proposed routes to recognizing an imam are prophetic designation, choice by qualified electors, or possession of the required qualities and self-assertion. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Proposed routes to recognizing an imam are prophetic designation, choice by qualified electors, or possession of the required qualities and self-assertion. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p3 Imamis say the imam is known only through a prophetic text; al-Qurtubi accepts reasoned consideration and qualified consensus as routes. — QURTUBI-FULL (الإمامية): Imamis say the imam is known only through a prophetic text; al-Qurtubi accepts reasoned consideration and qualified consensus as routes. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الإمامية)]
- 2:30/q25/p4 The imam has eleven conditions; the first is descent from Quraysh, whose supporting report continues in the next segment. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam has eleven conditions; the first is descent from Quraysh, whose supporting report continues in the next segment. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p5 One qualified member of the people of authority can conclude the imamate, against those who require a group; Umar's pledge of Abu Bakr was accepted by the companions, and contracts need no minimum number. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): One qualified member of the people of authority can conclude the imamate, against those who require a group; Umar's pledge of Abu Bakr was accepted by the companions, and contracts need no minimum number. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 2:30/q25/p6 Some count seizure by a qualified contender as a fourth route to the imamate, even without consultation or prior choice, once people pledge to him. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Some count seizure by a qualified contender as a fourth route to the imamate, even without consultation or prior choice, once people pledge to him. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p7 Ibn Khuwayz Mandad says a qualified person who seizes authority without consultation or choice has a completed pledge once people give allegiance. — QURTUBI-FULL (ابن خويز منداد): Ibn Khuwayz Mandad says a qualified person who seizes authority without consultation or choice has a completed pledge once people give allegiance. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(ابن خويز منداد)]
- 2:30/q25/p8 The first condition is Qurayshi descent, supported by the report that leaders are from Quraysh; the author notes disagreement over it. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The first condition is Qurayshi descent, supported by the report that leaders are from Quraysh; the author notes disagreement over it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p9 God's choosing Talut means selecting him and indicates lineage as a condition; the imam need not be infallible, know the unseen, be the best horseman or bravest, or belong only to Banu Hashim. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): God's choosing Talut means selecting him and indicates lineage as a condition; the imam need not be infallible, know the unseen, be the best horseman or bravest, or belong only to Banu Hashim. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p10 The imam must be fit to judge Muslims and be an independent jurist who can address cases without needing another's legal opinion; this is agreed. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam must be fit to judge Muslims and be an independent jurist who can address cases without needing another's legal opinion; this is agreed. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p11 He needs sound judgment and practical expertise in war, armies, frontier defense, protecting the community, restraining wrongdoing, and recovering victims' rights. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): He needs sound judgment and practical expertise in war, armies, frontier defense, protecting the community, restraining wrongdoing, and recovering victims' rights. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p12 He must not be too tenderhearted to impose penalties or afraid to execute severe punishments; the companions' consensus and the imam's judicial and supervisory duties support these qualifications. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): He must not be too tenderhearted to impose penalties or afraid to execute severe punishments; the companions' consensus and the imam's judicial and supervisory duties support these qualifications. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p13 The imam must be free and Muslim; the author says the requirements of freedom and Islam are clear. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam must be free and Muslim; the author says the requirements of freedom and Islam are clear. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p14 The imam must be male and physically sound; women cannot hold the imamate, although jurists differ on women's eligibility to judge cases in which their testimony is admissible. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam must be male and physically sound; women cannot hold the imamate, although jurists differ on women's eligibility to judge cases in which their testimony is admissible. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p15 The imam must be an adult and sane, with no disagreement on these requirements. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam must be an adult and sane, with no disagreement on these requirements. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p16 The imam must be just, and the community agrees a sinful person cannot initially be appointed; he should be foremost in knowledge, as the leaders' report and God's choosing Talut for knowledge and bodily strength indicate. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): The imam must be just, and the community agrees a sinful person cannot initially be appointed; he should be foremost in knowledge, as the leaders' report and God's choosing Talut for knowledge and bodily strength indicate. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p17 The imam should be male, free, adult, sane, Muslim, just, learned in independent reasoning, perceptive, physically sound, experienced in war and judgment, and Qurayshi according to the correct view; Hashimi descent and infallibility are not required, — IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): The imam should be male, free, adult, sane, Muslim, just, learned in independent reasoning, perceptive, physically sound, experienced in war and judgment, and Qurayshi according to the correct view; Hashimi descent and infallibility are not required, contrary to extremist Rafidites. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL]
- 2:30/q25/p18 The imamate may be established by the people qualified to bind and loose, or by one of them pledging allegiance; the majority requires adherence, and al-Juwayni reports consensus on this. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (الجمهور): The imamate may be established by the people qualified to bind and loose, or by one of them pledging allegiance; the majority requires adherence, and al-Juwayni reports consensus on this. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(الجمهور)]
- 2:30/q25/p19 An imam who gains power by compelling people to obey must be recognized to prevent division and disagreement; al-Shafi‘i explicitly states this. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (الشافعي): An imam who gains power by compelling people to obey must be recognized to prevent division and disagreement; al-Shafi‘i explicitly states this. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(الشافعي)]
- 2:30/q25/p20 One view says witnesses are not a condition for the imamate contract. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (بعضهم): One view says witnesses are not a condition for the imamate contract. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL(بعضهم)]
- 2:30/q25/p21 Political organization is needed to fulfill duties assigned to the community as a whole; individuals conditionally transfer their supra-individual authority and responsibility to a worthy person. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): Political organization is needed to fulfill duties assigned to the community as a whole; individuals conditionally transfer their supra-individual authority and responsibility to a worthy person. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]
- 2:30/q25/p22 The pledge of obedience to a caliph is conditional on the caliph following God's commands and prohibitions and the religious rules governing the office. — KURANYOLU-TEFSIR (author): The pledge of obedience to a caliph is conditional on the caliph following God's commands and prohibitions and the religious rules governing the office. [1 notes: KURANYOLU-TEFSIR]

## 2:30/q26 When may an imam be removed or resign?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · law]
- 2:30/q26/p1 A report from ʿAwf b. Malik says rulers are not to be opposed so long as they establish prayer; Muslim transmits it. — QURTUBI-FULL (النبي): A report from ʿAwf b. Malik says rulers are not to be opposed so long as they establish prayer; Muslim transmits it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(النبي)]
- 2:30/q26/p2 Umm Salama reports that one who inwardly dislikes and rejects wrongdoing by rulers is safe, while one who approves and follows is culpable; the Prophet says not to fight rulers while they pray, and Muslim transmits — QURTUBI-FULL (النبي): Umm Salama reports that one who inwardly dislikes and rejects wrongdoing by rulers is safe, while one who approves and follows is culpable; the Prophet says not to fight rulers while they pray, and Muslim transmits it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(النبي)]
- 2:30/q26/p3 An imam must resign if he finds a defect in himself that affects his fitness for office. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): An imam must resign if he finds a defect in himself that affects his fitness for office. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q26/p4 Jurists disagree whether an unimpaired imam may resign; the author says he may, citing Abu Bakr's request to be released, the companions' refusal, and the analogy of an agent or judge who can resign. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Jurists disagree whether an unimpaired imam may resign; the author says he may, citing Abu Bakr's request to be released, the companions' refusal, and the analogy of an agent or judge who can resign. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL+]
- 2:30/q26/p5 A revolt against an imam known for justice must be fought; if the imam is sinful and the rebel appears just, people should wait to assess him or agree collectively to remove the first imam, since — QURTUBI-FULL (author): A revolt against an imam known for justice must be fought; if the imam is sinful and the rebel appears just, people should wait to assess him or agree collectively to remove the first imam, since rebels may revert after gaining power. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q26/p6 A sinful imam is not deposed according to the sounder view, based on the Prophet’s instruction to tolerate him unless manifest unbelief is seen with proof from God. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): A sinful imam is not deposed according to the sounder view, based on the Prophet’s instruction to tolerate him unless manifest unbelief is seen with proof from God. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL+]
- 2:30/q26/p7 Whether an imam may resign is disputed; Hasan ibn Ali relinquished authority to Muawiyah for an excuse, and Ibn Kathir says he was praised for it. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (author): Whether an imam may resign is disputed; Hasan ibn Ali relinquished authority to Muawiyah for an excuse, and Ibn Kathir says he was praised for it. [1 notes: IBNKATHIR-FULL]

## 2:30/q27 May more than one imam rule simultaneously?  [خَلِيفَةًۭ · law]
- 2:30/q27/p1 If two caliphs receive pledges, the first remains caliph and the second is to be killed; the author says literal killing is clearer, though some understand it as removal. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): If two caliphs receive pledges, the first remains caliph and the second is to be killed; the author says literal killing is clearer, though some understand it as removal. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q27/p2 The Karramiyya argue that a pair of rulers each manages his own region and that two prophets coexisting would not invalidate prophethood, so two imams need not invalidate imamate. — QURTUBI-FULL (الكرامية): The Karramiyya argue that a pair of rulers each manages his own region and that two prophets coexisting would not invalidate prophethood, so two imams need not invalidate imamate. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الكرامية)]
- 2:30/q27/p3 A report from ʿAbd Allah b. ʿAmr says one who pledges hand and heart must obey if able, but a rival claimant is to be struck down; Muslim transmits it. — QURTUBI-FULL (النبي): A report from ʿAbd Allah b. ʿAmr says one who pledges hand and heart must obey if able, but a rival claimant is to be struck down; Muslim transmits it. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(النبي)]
- 2:30/q27/p4 A report from ʿArfaja says to strike a rival claimant with the sword, whoever he may be. — QURTUBI-FULL (النبي): A report from ʿArfaja says to strike a rival claimant with the sword, whoever he may be. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(النبي)]
- 2:30/q27/p5 Having two imams causes hypocrisy, opposition, schism, and loss of blessings; distant regions such as al-Andalus and Khurasan may be an exception discussed later. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Having two imams causes hypocrisy, opposition, schism, and loss of blessings; distant regions such as al-Andalus and Khurasan may be an exception discussed later. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q27/p6 Installing two or three imams at one time and in one city is unanimously forbidden. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Installing two or three imams at one time and in one city is unanimously forbidden. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q27/p7 Abu al-Maʿali says his colleagues bar two imams at opposite ends of the world; he personally finds a pair in one contiguous region forbidden by consensus while distant realms remain an open possibility. — QURTUBI-FULL (أبو المعالي): Abu al-Maʿali says his colleagues bar two imams at opposite ends of the world; he personally finds a pair in one contiguous region forbidden by consensus while distant realms remain an open possibility. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(أبو المعالي)]
- 2:30/q27/p8 Abu Ishaq permits two imams in extremely distant regions so people's rights and legal judgments are not left unattended. — QURTUBI-FULL (الأستاذ أبو إسحاق): Abu Ishaq permits two imams in extremely distant regions so people's rights and legal judgments are not left unattended. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL(الأستاذ أبو إسحاق)]
- 2:30/q27/p9 The Karramiyya permit two imams without qualification, arguing that each governs his own region and that two prophets could coexist; they take Ali and Muʿawiya to be two imams. — QURTUBI-FULL (الكرامية): The Karramiyya permit two imams without qualification, arguing that each governs his own region and that two prophets could coexist; they take Ali and Muʿawiya to be two imams. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q27/p11 Even if reason does not rule out two imams and no other text forbids them, consensus is decisive evidence that they are prohibited. — QURTUBI-FULL (author): Even if reason does not rule out two imams and no other text forbids them, consensus is decisive evidence that they are prohibited. [1 notes: QURTUBI-FULL]
- 2:30/q27/p12 The majority forbids appointing two or more imams in one realm, citing a prophetic command to kill anyone who tries to divide a united community; several authorities report consensus. — IBNKATHIR-FULL (الجمهور): The majority forbids appointing two or more imams in one realm, citing a prophetic command to kill anyone who tries to divide a united community; several authorities report consensus.
